# Full-domain two-channel spiral guarantees collapse to Huber

Status: COMPLETE JOINT ANALYTICAL CANDIDATE, UNVERIFIED.
Worker max2, assigned analytical round52. TASK.md and originating
master_guidance71ea82aa7acc7e5a authorize this one round, with all
computational and formal gates closed. Plan:ea2096c2f303efcc.
No fact_submit is authorized or made, and no new accepted fact is claimed.

This document defines a NEW scalar functional L_wide. It preserves the
old name L_sp and its restricted domain unchanged. All new algebra needed
below, including the reused hierarchy and mass-deletion ingredients, is
proved jointly here. The sole accepted mathematical input is
fact04219554e41d9750, for the original benchmark ray and saved enclosure.
The unverified geometric construction and independent analytical audit
are provenance, never correctness premises in this scalar proof.

The conclusion is an upper bound for two explicit lower-guarantee
formulas, not for the actual worst-case error W. It does not bound the
stronger exact closest-radius formula(6) of the geometric source, nor
the source-format realization after a closest-minimizer improvement.

## 1. Exact enlarged scalar definition

The immutable geometric source is finding c8fcf4ced5a34046 at
/opt/danus/runtime/projects/gd-exact-profile-20260926/research/shared_evidence/c8fcf4ced5a34046.md,
SHA2564d1e99db853eba3751a3f4761fc2c400c68eacdc9c7912b17c0b0c82d6975d2e.
Its formulas(1),(2),(7),(8) define the following numbers, without needing
any assertion about a function realizing them.

Take independently

    0<epsilon<=1/2, kappa>0.

For every such pair set

    theta_sp=arctan(1/epsilon),
    C=c_sp=sqrt(1+epsilon^2)*exp(kappa*theta_sp),
    d_sp=1-kappa^2,
    T_sp=(exp(d_sp*theta_sp/kappa)-1)/d_sp, 0<kappa<1,
    T_sp=theta_sp/kappa,                   kappa>=1,
    H_sp=C*[1+(1+kappa)*T_sp]-1,
    A=A_sp=epsilon+(H_sp+1)/epsilon,
    a_sp=2*A/C.                                           (1)

C and A are scoped abbreviations, not the accepted benchmark C_n or a
primitive-certificate capacity. The definition includes kappa=1 using
the second branch, with no division by d_sp there. Define exactly

    qmax_sp=1,                                       kappa<=epsilon,
    qmax_sp=sqrt((1+epsilon^2)/(1+kappa^2))
              *exp(kappa*[theta_sp-arctan(1/kappa)]), kappa>epsilon,
    N_sp=max{0,kappa*qmax_sp-epsilon},
    B_sp=1+max{epsilon^(-2),N_sp/epsilon}.                  (2)

No simplification of the general-kappa horizontal-normal price N_sp is
assumed. In particular, this definition does not silently substitute the
smaller old threshold when kappa>1.

Let h=(h_0,...,h_(n-1)) be any finite nonnegative word at its original
physical length n. A nonempty subset of its original physical indices,
listed in increasing order, induces the actual decomposition

    h=(block_0,b_0,block_1,b_1,...,block_(k-1),b_(k-1),block_k),

where k>=1 and s_i is the ordinary sum of block_i. Empty blocks and
zero physical entries are permitted. One parameter pair is fixed for
the entire selection. Define the two distinct scalar channels

    Phi_M=1/(1+2*s_k)*product_(i=0)^(k-1)
             [(b_i-1)/(C*s_i+max{epsilon*b_i,A})]^2,
             eligible when every b_i>=B_sp;                (3)

    Phi_S=1/[2*(1+s_k)]*product_(i=0)^(k-1)
             [b_i/(C*s_i+2*A+epsilon*b_i)]^2,
             eligible when every b_i>=2*(1+epsilon^(-2)).  (4)

Equation(4) uses C*(a_sp+s_i)=2*A+C*s_i exactly. Its numerator is b_i,
not b_i-1. Its terminal denominator is 2*(1+s_k), not 1+2*s_k.

Write H_base(h)=1/(1+2*sum h). Define

    L_wide(h)=max{H_base(h),
                 sup Phi_M over all pairs and eligible nonempty subsets,
                 sup Phi_S over all pairs and eligible nonempty subsets}. (5)

The supremum of an empty family in(5) means0. The supremum notation does
not presume parameter attainment. No product mixes factors from different
channels. The Huber term is included explicitly; hence the equality
proved below is attained by that term. All constants and denominators in
(1)--(5) are positive on their stated domains.

The source describes(4) as an exact payoff normalized by its specified
minimizer0 and initial distance1. We retain that convention. Interpreting
(3) or(4) as lower bounds for actual W remains conditional on establishing
the full geometry, which is not part of this scalar candidate.

## 2. Word family and the quantitative claims

Put lambda=1+sqrt(2). For a fixed real tau>0 and integer m>tau define

    beta=1-tau/m, c=lambda*(1+tau/m), a=beta/c,
    H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a*c^j,H_j), 0<=j<m,
    H_(m,tau)=H_m, n_m=2^m-1, S_j=sum H_j.                (6)

Every child keeps the SAME enclosing parameters(m,tau). A level is the
index j of a bridge 1+a*c^j, not the physical index of one occurrence.
Only tau=1 is the original diagnostic family; the theorem uses the
different words specified by small tau in(6).

The joint claims to be proved are as follows. For EVERY real
0<tau<=1/4, EVERY integer m>=1536, EVERY parameter pair in(1), and EVERY
nonempty subset of the physical word H_(m,tau), one has

    Phi_M<=1/(1+2*S_m),
    Phi_S<=5/[16*(1+S_m)].                               (7)

In(7) the formulas can even be evaluated on ineligible subsets; the
proof bounds that larger comparison class. Therefore(7) applies, in
particular, to each channel's own actual threshold, including equality.
Consequently

    L_wide(H_(m,tau))=1/(1+2*S_m).                       (8)

Retain the ORIGINAL all-split benchmark, defined for integers n>=0 by

    Omega(x,y)=(x+y+sqrt((x+y)^2+4*x*y))/2,
    G(x,y)=2*x+y/2+sqrt(y^2+8*x*y)/2,
    C_0=B_0=1,
    C_n=max_(0<=i<n) Omega(C_i,C_(n-i-1)),
    B_n=max_(0<=i<n) G(C_i,B_(n-i-1)),
    U_n^ZJ=1/B_n.                                      (9)

Accepted fact04219554e41d9750 gives the positive finite limit

    B_(2^m-1)/lambda^m -> Z_1.                         (10)

For every fixed0<tau<=1/4 the further claim is

    lim_(m->infinity) B_(n_m)*L_wide(H_(m,tau))
                         =Z_1/(2*exp(tau)).            (11)

In fact, the strict benchmark deficits already hold at EVERY m>=1536:

    B_(n_m)*L_wide(H_(m,1/4))<237/250<1,
    B_(n_m)*L_wide(H_(m,tau))<79/80 for1/5<=tau<=1/4.  (11a)

The corresponding limits are strictly below the same constants. These
claims use only the accepted benchmark enclosure, rederived from its
saved endpoint in Section8. No new benchmark calculation is performed.

## 3. Joint hierarchy identities and the selection mechanism

For fixed(m,tau), put

    r=c*(c-2), v=a/(c-2)=beta/r,
    M_j=1+S_j, d_j=a*c^j, b_j=1+d_j,
    u_j=d_j/M_j, M_max=M_(m-1).                         (12)

Writing x=tau/m and gamma=4+2*sqrt(2), direct expansion gives

    r=1+gamma*x+lambda^2*x^2>1>beta>0.                 (13)

Thus0<v<1 and c>lambda>2. The sum recurrence is
M_(j+1)=2*M_j+a*c^j with M_0=1. Substitution, using a=v*(c-2), proves
by induction that

    M_j=v*c^j+(1-v)*2^j, 0<=j<=m.                     (14)

It follows that

    1<=M_j<=c^j,
    0<u_j<=c-2,
    M_j<=M_max for0<=j<m.                              (15)

For the first two inequalities use c>2 and M_j>=v*c^j. For the last,
the sum recurrence has positive added terms and M_j>0.

Choose ANY currently selected checkpoint having minimum selected level
j. Its immediate complete H_j children contain only levels<j, so neither
child contains a selected checkpoint. If s and t are the actual ordinary
sums immediately before and after this checkpoint, then

    s>=S_j, t>=S_j.                                   (16)

This includes a first or last selected checkpoint: its adjacent ordinary
block is then the true initial or terminal block. Tied minimum levels
cause no problem because each child has strictly smaller levels.

Deleting a checkpoint below means deleting it from the selected subset,
not from the physical word. Its full physical step b is reclassified as
ordinary, so the two ordinary blocks merge to the sum s+b+t. All other
physical indices, step values, and the one parameter pair are retained.
Eligibility of any remaining checkpoint is unchanged.

We prove one minimum-level deletion scheme for both channels under the
same whole-word alternatives

    (I)  C>=r=c*(c-2),
    (II) A>=2*C*M_max,                                (17)

assuming C>=1, A>=2*C and2<c<=5/2. Section7 proves these assumptions
and the dichotomy for the ENTIRE enlarged parameter domain. The two
channel comparisons below are separate; neither is inferred from a
pointwise ordering of(3) and(4).

## 4. Mass channel: exact interior and terminal comparisons

Let b=b_j=1+d be a selected minimum-level checkpoint, with
M=M_j, S=M-1, u=d/M. Then0<u<=1/2 and s,t>=S.
For this channel write Q_M(b)=max{epsilon*b,A}.

If a next selected checkpoint b' exists, put R=Q_M(b'). Cancelling its
positive numerator b'-1 and all unchanged factors shows that deletion
does not lower Phi_M exactly when

    d*[C*(s+b+t)+R] <= (C*s+Q_M(b))*(C*t+R).           (18)

The right side minus the left is increasing in Q_M(b). Its derivatives
with respect to R,s,t are, respectively,

    C*s+Q_M(b)-d,
    C*(C*t+R-d),
    C*(C*s+Q_M(b)-d).

They are positive throughout s,t>=M-1 and Q_M(b),R>=A: indeed
C*(M-1)+A>=C*M>=M>=2*d. Thus its minimum is at
s=t=M-1 and Q_M(b)=R=A. Put z_M=(A-C)/M>=0. Dividing that minimum by
M^2 gives the exact expression

    E_M=C^2-C*u*(2+u)+z_M*(2*C-u)+z_M^2.              (19)

Under(I), u*(2+u)<=c*(c-2)<=C, so every grouped term in(19) is
nonnegative. Under(II),

    z_M=(A-C)/M>=C*(2*M_max-1)/M>=C,

because M<=M_max and M_max>=1. Therefore

    E_M>=C^2-(5/4)*C+C*(2*C-1/2)+C^2
        =4*C^2-(7/4)*C>0.                            (20)

Here u*(2+u)<=5/4 and2*C-u>0 justify each replacement. This proves
the interior deletion comparison in either branch of(17).

If b is the LAST selected checkpoint, the exact comparison is instead

    d^2*[1+2*(s+b+t)]
      <=(C*s+Q_M(b))^2*(1+2*t).                      (21)

Since C*s+Q_M(b)>=s+1, it suffices to bound

    d^2*[1+2*(s+b+t)]/[(s+1)^2*(1+2*t)].              (22)

For fixed s this decreases in t, because the fraction without d^2 is
[1+2*(s+b)/(1+2*t)]/(s+1)^2. Its s derivative after suppressing the
positive constant1+2*t is -2*(s+2*b+2*t)/(s+1)^3<0. Hence(22) is
largest at s=t=M-1. There it equals

    u^2*[(4+2*u)*M-1]/(2*M-1)
      <=u^2*(3+2*u)<=1.                              (23)

For the first inequality, the difference of the cross-multiplied
numerators is2*(1+u)*(M-1)>=0. For the second, u^2*(3+2*u) increases
on[0,1/2] and equals1 at1/2. This terminal comparison needs no
additional choice between(I) and(II).

Repeatedly deleting a currently selected minimum level strictly reduces
the number of selections. The comparisons keep Phi_M nondecreasing
until the empty-selection value1/(1+2*S_m) is reached. This proves
the first inequality in(7) under(17). It is a proof for an arbitrary
finite subset, not an executed recurrence or enumeration.

## 5. Source-format channel: the numerator shift is paid explicitly

Keep b=1+d, M=M_j, u=d/M, and s,t>=M-1 from Section4. Now define

    Q_S(b)=2*A+epsilon*b.

Its numerator is b itself. If a next selected checkpoint b' exists,
write R=Q_S(b'). The exact interior deletion comparison, after cancelling
the unchanged positive b', is

    b*[C*(s+b+t)+R] <= (C*s+Q_S(b))*(C*t+R).           (24)

The difference is increasing in Q_S(b), and its R,s,t derivatives are

    C*s+Q_S(b)-b,
    C*(C*t+R-b),
    C*(C*s+Q_S(b)-b),

respectively. All are positive on the larger comparison region
s,t>=M-1, Q_S(b),R>=2*A. In fact,

    C*(M-1)+2*A-b
      >=C*M+3*C-(M/2+1)>0,                           (25)

using A>=2*C, C>=1, M>=1 and d<=M/2. Therefore the smallest difference
in(24) occurs at s=t=M-1 and Q_S(b)=R=2*A. Define

    q=2*A-C>=3*C.

The minimum is EXACTLY

    F_S=(C*M+q)^2-(d+1)*[C*(2*M+d)+q]
       =M^2*[C^2-C*u*(2+u)]
         +M*[q*(2*C-u)-C*(2+u)]+q^2-q.               (26)

For the first equality, note that the merged physical sum is
2*(M-1)+(d+1)=2*M+d-1, so adding R=2*A yields
C*(2*M+d)+q. The extra1 in b=d+1 is retained in every term of(26).

Under(I), the M^2 coefficient in(26) is nonnegative. Also

    q*(2*C-u)-C*(2+u)
      >=3*C*(2*C-u)-C*(2+u)
       =C*(6*C-4*u-2)>=2*C>0,                       (27)

and q^2-q>=0 because q>=3*C>=3. Thus(24) holds in branch(I).

Under(II), use the first expression in(26). Put z_S=q/M. Since

    q=2*A-C>=C*(4*M_max-1)>=3*C*M,

one has z_S>=3*C. Moreover(d+1)/M<=3/2 and(2*M+d)/M<=5/2. Consequently

    F_S/M^2
      >=(C+z_S)^2-(3/2)*[(5/2)*C+z_S]
       =C^2-(15/4)*C+z_S*(2*C-3/2)+z_S^2
      >=16*C^2-(33/4)*C>0.                          (28)

The last step uses C>=1, so2*C-3/2>0 and substitution z_S>=3*C has
the claimed direction. Its final expression is positive for C>=1.
This proves the interior source-format comparison for both branches.

For a LAST selected checkpoint, deletion replaces the terminal factor
1/[2*(1+t)] by1/[2*(1+s+b+t)]. The exact ratio of the selected
local factor to the deleted local factor is

    R_tail=b^2*(1+s+b+t)/[(C*s+Q_S(b))^2*(1+t)].       (29)

Because Q_S(b)>=2*A>=4*C and C>=1, its denominator satisfies
C*s+Q_S(b)>=s+4. The resulting upper bound for(29) is

    b^2*(1+s+b+t)/[(s+4)^2*(1+t)].                   (30)

For fixed s, the fraction without b^2 equals
[1+(s+b)/(1+t)]/(s+4)^2 and decreases with t. Its derivative in s,
with the positive constant1+t suppressed, is

    (2-s-2*b-2*t)/(s+4)^3<0,

since b=1+d>1. Thus(30) is largest at s=t=M-1, where

    R_tail
      <=(d+1)^2*(2*M+d)/[(M+3)^2*M]
      <=(5/2)*[(M/2+1)/(M+3)]^2
      <=5/8.                                       (31)

For the last two inequalities use d<=M/2 and
M/2+1<=(M+3)/2. This is a uniform quantitative terminal contraction.
It has not been replaced by the mass-channel terminal comparison.

## 6. Finite pruning and the two different empty-selection values

For the source channel only, define the empty-product bookkeeping value
to be1/[2*(1+S_m)]. This is the value of its own displayed formula after
all checkpoints are removed from the subset. It is a comparison sink;
the functional(5) retains its actual Huber baseline1/(1+2*S_m).

Start with any nonempty selected subset, and repeatedly choose a minimum
currently selected level. At an interior deletion use(24); at a terminal
deletion use(31). Every remaining subset still has the complete children
property(16). At least one deletion is terminal, since the last remaining
checkpoint is terminal. All factors outside the local comparison are
unchanged and positive. Multiplying the comparisons therefore gives

    Phi_S<=(5/8)*1/[2*(1+S_m)]
         =5/[16*(1+S_m)].                            (32)

If additional deletions are terminal, each contributes another factor
at most5/8; only one such factor was needed in(32). The mass pruning in
Section4 gives its own bound1/(1+2*S_m). Finally

    5/[16*(1+S_m)]<1/(1+2*S_m),                      (33)

because cross multiplication reduces to5*(1+2*S_m)<16*(1+S_m).
Thus under the hypotheses and dichotomy(17), both channels are bounded
by the Huber baseline, and that baseline attains L_wide.

No pointwise comparison between Phi_S and Phi_M was used. The extra1
in the source numerator is absorbed in the q-dependent terms(26)--(28),
and the terminal factor is handled by(29)--(31). In particular, merely
copying the old b-1 proof would not prove the source-channel assertion.

The proof also covers level0, whose children are empty and M_0=1,
and arbitrary ties between selected levels. The bounds were proved on
the larger class of all nonempty subsets of this positive word, so there
is no lost threshold case, including B_sp at kappa>1 or equality at
either actual eligibility threshold. No ordinary block is replaced by
an envelope, alternative ordering, or child with a new parameter m.

## 7. The deletion dichotomy holds on the entire enlarged domain

Assume0<tau<=1/4 and m>=1536. First2<c<5/2. To give rational bounds,
sqrt(2)<10/7 implies lambda<17/7; for m>=9,

    c<=lambda*(1+1/(4*m))
      <(17/7)*(37/36)=629/252<5/2.                   (34)

Also gamma=4+2*sqrt(2)<7 and lambda^2=3+2*sqrt(2)<6, so m>=2 and(13)
give

    r-1<7/(4*m)+3/(8*m^2)
       <=31/(16*m)<2/m.                              (35)

For EVERY pair0<epsilon<=1/2,kappa>0, one has C>1. Both branches of
T_sp in(1) are positive. Therefore, without any restriction kappa<1,

    A=epsilon+(C/epsilon)*[1+(1+kappa)*T_sp]
      >=C/epsilon>=2*C.                              (36)

This includes kappa=1 and every kappa>1. The source's piecewise T_sp
and threshold price remain unchanged.

If C>=r, alternative(I) applies immediately. Consider ANY remaining
pair with C<r. Since C>=sqrt(1+epsilon^2), equations(35) and m>=2 imply

    epsilon^2<=C^2-1<r^2-1
      <4/m+4/m^2<=6/m<=1/256.                       (37)

Thus epsilon<1/16. Using pi>3 and arctan(epsilon)<epsilon gives

    theta_sp=pi/2-arctan(epsilon)>23/16>1.            (38)

For completeness pi>3 follows by comparing the unit circle circumference
with its inscribed regular hexagon, and arctan(epsilon)<epsilon follows
by integrating1/(1+t^2)<1 for positive t. Neither uses a decimal value.

The SAME assumption C<r yields, directly from the formula for C,

    kappa*theta_sp<=log C<log r<r-1<2/m.              (39)

Since theta_sp>1 and m>=1536, this implies

    kappa<2/m<=1/768<1/16.

In particular kappa<1 has now been proved in precisely the branch
where the exponential expression for T_sp will be used. Put

    X=(1-kappa^2)*theta_sp/kappa.

From(38)--(39),

    X>((1-kappa^2)*theta_sp^2/2)*m
      >(255/256)*(529/256)*(m/2)
       =(134895/131072)*m>m.                         (40)

For0<kappa<1, the formula in(1) simplifies exactly to

    1+(1+kappa)*T_sp=(exp(X)-kappa)/(1-kappa)
                    >=exp(X).                       (41)

Indeed subtracting exp(X) gives
kappa*(exp(X)-1)/(1-kappa)>=0. Combining(37),(40),(41) yields

    A>=C*exp(X)/epsilon>16*C*exp(m).                  (42)

The positive exponential series gives exp(1)>1+1+1/2=5/2>c.
Together with(15),

    M_max<=c^(m-1)<exp(m),
    A>16*C*exp(m)>2*C*M_max.                         (43)

This is alternative(II). Hence every parameter pair is covered by
(I) or(II), and Sections4--6 prove(7)--(8).

This is a finite uniform argument: no limits or optimized constants
K=m*kappa and E=m*epsilon were assumed. It includes every horizon-
dependent scaling, oscillatory choices, fixed positive curvature, both
sides of kappa=epsilon, both sides of kappa=1, and epsilon=1/2. Within
the stated small-tau family there is no remaining parameter region.

The precise changes from the old-domain proof are now visible. Its
small-C branch used kappa<=epsilon<1/16 to bound1-kappa^2. Here(39)
forces kappa<1/16 directly. It also used its exponential formula for A
throughout the old domain. Here the universal bound(36) replaces that
use outside the small-C branch, and(39) independently justifies the
exponential branch wherever(41) is used. No ordering of kappa and epsilon
enters any deletion comparison. The new source-channel comparisons in
Section5 additionally pay its different numerator and terminal factor.

## 8. Original benchmark: saved endpoint, finite deficit, and exact limit

Only the following part of accepted fact04219554e41d9750 is used:
the original all-split definitions(9), the positive ray limit(10), and
the unconditional enclosure

    lambda^(-j)*B_(2^j-1)<=Z_1
      <=lambda^(-j)*(sqrt(B_(2^j))+3/100)^2, j>=0.     (44)

Its conditional four-step seed construction is not a premise. The
relevant statement and Sections7--8 of that accepted fact were read.
Its Section8 fixes the saved table
/opt/danus/runtime/projects/gd-exact-profile-20260926/research/audit_c78/input_interval_table.csv,
SHA2562a7585bc0bade9f85461456c13d60bbe618f85c7cc816e1ddc5b4c1a1d611e68.
The hash and saved rows1023,1024 were read back; neither the generator
nor any benchmark or exact-check program was executed.

With D80=1208925819614629174706176, its row1024 gives

    D80*B_1024<=H1024=19229705732238461978760749948
                    <15910*D80
                     =19234009790068750169575260160. (45)

The integer comparison is displayed to expose the exact saved input,
not as a newly computed benchmark. Since19600<19602, sqrt(2)>140/99.
The binomial identity gives

    lambda^10=3363+2378*sqrt(2)>6725,                 (46)

where(3363*99+2378*140)-6725*99=82>0. Apply(44) at j=10. Using(45),
(46), sqrt(15910)<127 and the written rational comparisons

    15910+381/50+9/10000<15918,
    15918*100=1591800<1593825=6725*237,

one obtains

    Z_1<(sqrt(15910)+3/100)^2/6725
        <15918/6725<237/100.                         (47)

This rederives the already available rational enclosure directly from
the accepted fact and its fixed endpoint. No unverified sibling's
derived value of Z_1 is used as an input.

Here is an explicit finite comparison, strengthening a limit-only
statement. For EVERY0<tau<=1/4 and m>=1536, equations(12),(35) imply

    v=beta/r>(1-1/(4*m))/(1+2/m)
             =1-[9/(4*m)]/(1+2/m)
             >1-9/(4*m)>255/256.                    (48)

For the last inequality,9/(4*m)<=3/2048<8/2048=1/256. Also lambda>2
gives lambda^(-m)<1/256. From(14), the exact normalized physical mass is

    (1+2*S_m)/lambda^m
       =2*v*(1+tau/m)^m+2*(1-v)*(2/lambda)^m
           -lambda^(-m).                            (49)

All terms in the second summand are nonnegative. At tau=1/4, the first
three nonnegative binomial terms and m>=2 give

    (1+1/(4*m))^m
      >=1+1/4+(m-1)/(32*m)>=81/64.

Therefore(48)--(49) imply

    (1+2*S_m)/lambda^m
      >2*(255/256)*(81/64)-1/256
       =20623/8192>5/2.                              (50)

The final comparison is20623>20480. Since(44) also gives
B_(n_m)/lambda^m<=Z_1, combining(8),(47),(50) proves

    B_(n_m)*L_wide(H_(m,1/4))<237/250
                      for EVERY integer m>=1536.    (51)

For the entire interval1/5<=tau<=1/4, the same binomial argument yields

    (1+tau/m)^m
      >=1+tau+[(m-1)/(2*m)]*tau^2
      >=1+1/5+1/100=121/100.

Hence, uniformly over that tau interval,

    (1+2*S_m)/lambda^m
      >2*(255/256)*(121/100)-1/256
       =6161/2560>12/5,                              (52)

since6161>6144. Equations(8),(44),(47),(52) give the second finite bound

    B_(n_m)*L_wide(H_(m,tau))<79/80
       for EVERY1/5<=tau<=1/4 and integer m>=1536.    (53)

No new benchmark horizon or numerical tail estimate is needed for these
finite claims. The physical index remains exactly n_m=2^m-1.

For completeness, for a fixed0<tau<=1/4, beta and r both tend1 as
m increases, so v tends1. The binomial limit
(1+tau/m)^m->exp(tau) can be read directly from

    tau/(1+tau/m)<=m*log(1+tau/m)<=tau,

which follows by integrating1/(1+t) between0 and tau/m. In(49), the
second summand tends0 since0<1-v<1 and2/lambda<1; the last term also
tends0. Thus

    (1+2*S_m)/lambda^m ->2*exp(tau).                  (54)

Combine(8),(10),(54) to get(11). Strict limiting constants follow from
(47) and exp(1/4)>5/4, or exp(tau)>=exp(1/5)>6/5 for tau>=1/5.
These strict inequalities follow from positive higher terms of the
exponential series. Thus both the finite and limiting comparisons have
the stated strict margin.

## 9. Review, retrieval, and exact scope

The following stress tests were checked symbolically in this ONE joint
candidate, without mathematical computation or a separate verifier:

* Every original nonempty subset is addressed by its own minimum selected
  level; no record-chain optimality is assumed.
* A checkpoint can be first, last, sole, tied in level, or at level0.
  Initial and terminal blocks still contain the stated complete children.
* Removing a selection merges s+b+t and keeps the full physical b=1+d.
  Interior next-checkpoint costs remain their ACTUAL values until the
  proved monotonicity lowers them to the comparison threshold.
* Both interior differences were expanded with their own numerators.
  The source-format extra1 gives the negative terms visible in(26);
  it is not discarded as a negligible large-level error.
* The two terminal denominators remain separate. The source comparison
  has the quantitative factor5/8, while the mass comparison is(21)--(23).
* All costs are kept positive at empty children and empty ordinary blocks.
  Threshold equalities and the general-kappa normal price are retained.
* No interchange of a parameter supremum and a limit occurs. Finite
  inequalities(37)--(43) cover the entire enlarged parameter domain.
* The parameters of every child remain the enclosing(m,tau). All benchmark
  comparisons use the original physical length and all-split recurrence.

## 10. Final theorem of the joint candidate

THEOREM (UNVERIFIED analytical candidate). Let L_wide be exactly(1)--(5),
including the piecewise T_sp, the mass threshold B_sp with its full N_sp
price, and the separate source threshold2*(1+epsilon^(-2)). Let H_(m,tau)
be exactly(6), with unchanged enclosing(m,tau) in every child. For every
real0<tau<=1/4 and integer m>=1536, simultaneously for all kappa>0,
0<epsilon<=1/2 and all respective eligible nonempty chronological
checkpoint subsets,

    Phi_M<=1/(1+2*S_m),
    Phi_S<=5/[16*(1+S_m)],
    L_wide(H_(m,tau))=1/(1+2*S_m).

For the original all-split benchmark(9) at the actual physical length
n_m=2^m-1, every such m satisfies

    B_(n_m)*L_wide(H_(m,1/4))<237/250<1.

Uniformly for all1/5<=tau<=1/4 and the same m>=1536 one also has

    B_(n_m)*L_wide(H_(m,tau))<79/80<1.

For each fixed0<tau<=1/4 the exact limit is

    lim_(m->infinity) B_(n_m)*L_wide(H_(m,tau))
                      =Z_1/(2*exp(tau)),

where Z_1 is the accepted positive benchmark ray limit from
fact04219554e41d9750. These are statements only about the defined scalar

formula(6), or its second realization after closest-radius normalization.

PROOF. The same minimum-level selection deletion mechanism in
Sections3--6 establishes both bounds under(17), paying each channel's
own numerator and terminal factor. Section7 proves(17) for every allowed
parameter pair at every stated depth. The baseline attains the equality.

# R4 handoff

Status: original exact GD minimax target OPEN. No complete candidate for formal review; all new mathematics is an unverified analytical draft.

Read PROOF_ATTEMPT.md for the self-contained statements and proofs. This was one native analytical invocation, with two substantive proof lines, mathematical PROGRAM0, numerical/array/solver0, formal0, and no additional agent/model. No worker, service, truth-store, Goal, publication or finalization action was taken.

## What was actually constructed

1. A fixed finite-dimensional function pasting two complete projection-type GD histories using one auxiliary coordinate. Equations (4)--(10) specify all points, gradients, values and cross-history rows; (11) pays the original initial radius. The full function is the quadratic-regularized support function of one finite gradient hull. It is chosen once before the run.
2. A fixed convex 1-smooth function realizing a terminal transverse-gradient perturbation of a strictly quadratic prefix. Its necessary and sufficient finite interpolation budget is (18), and (19) is an explicit conjugate construction, including an attained minimum. This is only a realized prefix, not a completed adverse trajectory for the original problem.
3. Within the same second proof line, an actual shared-history pasting with no auxiliary initial radius: a rotated quadratic prefix satisfying (22), followed by the metric cap (23)--(24). Equations (25)--(28) check every old and cross row, give the original-radius payoff, and give explicit scalar parameter conditions. This is a full-word hard function under those conditions, not a universal all-word construction.

## Decisive checks

The first construction cannot itself provide the sharp target uniformly. On two nonzero quadratic Silver children, its generated errors have an all-depth ceiling

\[
\limsup_m B_{2^{m+1}-1} E_m\le2(\sqrt2-1)<1.
\]

This uses the exact benchmark recurrence and the analytic coarse estimate \(B_n\le4(n+1)^\rho\), not numerical recurrence evaluation. The polar-cone check is included: for a nonzero negative clock gradient the minimizer is unique, and at zero negative clock gradient 0 remains the closest minimizer. The ceiling is therefore not an artifact of selecting a distant minimizer.

Scope is crucial: this ceiling concerns the displayed pasted witnesses. It is not a bound on \(W_n\), and it does not exclude a larger proof strategy that selects a global Huber or quadratic witness on these same words. Do not record it as an obstruction to the original conjecture or to every conceivable static gluing.

The second line directly attacked the auxiliary-radius loss by sharing coordinates. An already fixed unit-curvature prefix can force the next bridge and a unit step to land at the minimum, so no unrestricted extension theorem can use an arbitrary such child history. A transverse endpoint perturbation is realizable only within the terminal-scale slack (18); an initial-radius estimate cannot substitute for that slack. Changing the whole shared history with two quadratic modes avoids that endpoint-only restriction. The actual cap in (22)--(28) does complete a compatible whole trajectory without adding radius. Its available prefix test requires a unique spectral sign-changing earlier step larger than the selected bridge, and its moment interval excludes a unit bridge. Arbitrary-word availability, recursive compatibility and the exact benchmark payoff remain unproved.

## What remains missing

There is no construction selecting compatible shared histories for every nonnegative word, no proven arbitrary-word split, and no uniform loss sum producing \(1-o(1)\) with the exact \(C_n/B_n\) recurrence. The optional negative direction did not produce a full-function upper certificate or asymptotic separation. The gap has the strength of the actual target; it is not an unproved technical lemma.

No formal review or automatic renewal is requested by this handoff. The one-call/two-line allocation is finished. Parent owns shared registration and administrative closeout.

<!-- BEGIN_SOURCE_BODY DOC-003406 -->
# Direct closure attempt R4: static pasting and its unresolved radius tradeoff

Date: 2026-10-08. Status: UNVERIFIED analytical draft; original target OPEN.

This invocation used two substantive lines: an actual static pasting with one auxiliary coordinate, then a shared-coordinate replacement. It produced no complete affirmative or negative proof. The first construction below is an admissible hard function, with its original radius and every cross-history row paid explicitly. Its quantified failure concerns that construction, not the original conjecture or a family augmented by other witnesses. The second line did not produce a uniform all-word pasting.

No mathematical program, numerical evaluation, array, solver, additional model, or formal submission was used. Text and primary-source reads and manual derivation were used. No truth store was changed.

## 1. Target and normalization

For a nonnegative predetermined word \(h=(h_0,\ldots,h_{n-1})\), GD is

\[
x_{i+1}=x_i-h_i\nabla f(x_i)
\]

after setting \(L=1\). The supremum defining \(W_n(h)\) is over all finite dimensions, convex differentiable functions with 1-Lipschitz gradient and an attained minimum, every selected minimizer, and every nonzero initial radius:

\[
W_n(h)=\sup\frac{2(f(x_n)-f_\star)}{\|x_0-x_\star\|^2}.
\]

Let \(C_0=B_0=1\), and, for every integer split \(k+l=n-1\), let

\[
\Omega(x,y)=\frac{x+y+\sqrt{x^2+6xy+y^2}}2,
\quad
G(x,y)=2x+\frac y2+\frac{\sqrt{y^2+8xy}}2,
\]

\[
C_n=\max_{k+l=n-1}\Omega(C_k,C_l),
\qquad B_n=\max_{k+l=n-1}G(C_k,B_l).
\]

The required affirmative result is

\[
\forall\epsilon>0\ \exists N_\epsilon\ \forall n\ge N_\epsilon\
\forall h\in[0,\infty)^n:
\quad W_n(h)\ge\frac{1-\epsilon}{B_n},
\]

with \(N_\epsilon\) independent of \(h\). A negative resolution would instead require a rigorous full-function upper certificate with a fixed asymptotic improvement over \(1/B_n\). Neither was obtained.

The source examined was Zhang--Jiang, [v2](https://arxiv.org/html/2410.12395v2), especially Sections 2.2, 3.1 and 3.2, Lemma 15 and the retained square in the dominant composition. These prove sufficient compositions; their reversal was not assumed. Proposition 24, also checked in that source, gives \(C_n\le(n+1)^\rho\), where \(\rho=\log_2(1+\sqrt2)>1\).

## 2. Calibration checked independently

For a word split at \(\alpha>1\), write \(x,y\) for the masses before and after the bridge, \(C=1+x\), \(b=\alpha-1\). The single fixed function with derivative

\[
f'(z)=\operatorname{clip}(z,-r,1),\qquad f(0)=0,
\]

started at \(C\), reaches 1 before the bridge and \(-b\) after it. With \(r=b/(2y+1)\), its whole suffix remains on the negative affine ray and

\[
\frac{2f(z_n)}{C^2}=\frac{b^2}{C^2(2y+1)}.
\]

Balancing this with the whole-word Huber witness \(1/[2C+2b+(2y+1)]\) yields

\[
(b+C)\{2b^2+(2y+1)b-C(2y+1)\}=0.
\]

The positive root is the stated \(\psi\) bridge. With \(r=b/(y+1)\), the terminal is \(-r\), the rows \(Q_{i,\star}=f_i-f_\star-\langle g_i,x_i-x_\star\rangle+\|g_i\|^2/2\) are zero, and the primitive retained energy is

\[
\frac12\{C^2-(x+\alpha+y+1)^2r^2\}.
\]

Its zero is \(b(C+(y+1)+b)=C(y+1)\), the stated \(\varphi\) bridge. These are calibration, not a converse or a recursive construction.

## 3. Line 1: an actual static pasting of two complete histories

### 3.1 A concrete finite-dimensional function

For a finite set of gradients whose convex hull (K) contains 0, define

\[
F_K(X)=\max_{g\in K}\{\langle X,g\rangle-\tfrac12\|g\|^2\}.
\tag{1}
\]

The maximizing gradient is (P_KX). Its uniqueness gives differentiability, and the projection inequalities give

\[
\|P_KX-P_KY\|^2\le\langle X-Y,P_KX-P_KY\rangle.
\]

Thus (1) is convex and 1-smooth. Also (F_K\ge0=F_K(0)). A proposed jet ((X_i,g_i)) is realized, with value (\langle X_i,g_i\rangle-\|g_i\|^2/2), precisely when

\[
\langle X_i-g_i,g-g_i\rangle\le0\quad(g\in K).
\tag{2}
\]

It suffices to check (2) at all generating gradients, including 0. This gives one fixed function after the whole word has been selected; it is not an adaptive reselection.

### 3.2 Inputs and the pasted trajectory

Let the prefix word have length \(k\), mass \(\sigma\), and partial masses \(s_i\), \(0\le i\le k\). Let its complete projection-type history be \((u_i,a_i)\), including its terminal gradient. Let the suffix word have length \(l\), mass \(\tau\), partial masses \(t_j\), and complete history \((v_j,b_j)\), \(0\le j\le l\). Both histories satisfy GD and (2), with their own 0 included. Their values are denoted \(F_A(u_i)\), \(F_B(v_j)\).

Fix any bridge \(\alpha>1\), and put \(d=\alpha-1\). Choose

\[
r\ge0,\quad\theta\ge0,\quad
\theta+(\tau+1)r\le d,\quad p>0,
\qquad T=(\sigma+1+\theta)p.
\tag{3}
\]

In the orthogonal space of dimension (d_A+d_B+1), prescribe the prefix jets

\[
X_i=(u_i,v_0,T-s_ip),\qquad A_i=(a_i,0,p),
\tag{4}
\]

and suffix jets

\[
Y_j=(c,v_j,(\theta-d+t_jr)p),\qquad
D_j=(0,b_j,-rp),\qquad c=u_k-\alpha a_k.
\tag{5}
\]

Each block has exactly its prescribed GD recursion, and

\[
X_k-\alpha A_k=Y_0.
\]

The coordinates called inactive here really are frozen along the displayed trajectory: their gradients are 0 in the relevant block. This is enforced by the global interpolation below, not assumed from a direct sum.

### 3.3 Every old row, cross row, and minimizer row

Take (K=\operatorname{conv}(\{0\}\cup\{A_i\}\cup\{D_j\})). Within each block, its projection rows are exactly the original child rows; the added gradient coordinate is constant. The rows against 0 hold because the original rows hold and the clock products are nonnegative by (3).

For a prefix normal against a suffix gradient, (2) is exactly

\[
-\langle u_i-a_i,a_i\rangle+\langle v_0,b_j\rangle
-(\sigma-s_i+\theta)(1+r)p^2\le0.
\tag{6}
\]

For a suffix normal against a prefix gradient it is exactly

\[
\langle c,a_i\rangle-\langle v_j-b_j,b_j\rangle
-[d-\theta-(t_j+1)r](1+r)p^2\le0.
\tag{7}
\]

The original minimizer rows imply that both subtracted child inner products are nonnegative. Consequently define

\[
M_1=\max(0,\max_j\langle v_0,b_j\rangle),\qquad
M_2=\max(0,\max_i\langle c,a_i\rangle),\qquad M=\max(M_1,M_2).
\]

The explicit choice

\[
\theta=d/4,\qquad r=\frac d{2(\tau+1)},\qquad
p^2\ge\frac{4M}{d(1+r)},\quad p>0
\tag{8}
\]

satisfies all rows: each clock margin in (6)--(7) is at least (d(1+r)p^2/4). Thus (1), with this finite (K), realizes the full pasted history.

The child function values are not silently reset. The exact global values are

\[
F_K(X_i)=F_A(u_i)+(\sigma-s_i+\theta+\tfrac12)p^2,
\tag{9}
\]

\[
F_K(Y_j)=F_B(v_j)+[r(d-\theta)-(t_j+\tfrac12)r^2]p^2.
\tag{10}
\]

Thus the old child contributions and all their internal interpolation rows are retained, with an explicitly specified lift of the global values. The displayed global data are fixed before any step and remain unchanged afterwards. This is not a zero-cost pasting of the original unlifted jets.

### 3.4 The actual original-radius lower witness

Using the valid selected minimizer 0, the original radius and terminal value give

\[
W_{k+l+1}(h_A,\alpha,h_B)\ge
\frac{2F_B(v_l)+2[r(d-\theta)-(\tau+\tfrac12)r^2]p^2}
{\|u_0\|^2+\|v_0\|^2+(\sigma+1+\theta)^2p^2}.
\tag{11}
\]

Zero steps inside either child cause no difficulty in the formulas. Children may be empty. The construction accepts arbitrarily large finite bridges (\alpha>1). It does not prescribe an arbitrary-word decomposition, and (11) has not been shown to match the exact (B_n) recursion.

### 3.5 An all-depth audit of this particular pasting

The auxiliary radius is a real loss, not just an overly distant selected minimizer, on the following family.

Write (\lambda=1+\sqrt2), (q=\sqrt2-1=1/\lambda), and (c_m=\lambda^m). Define the Silver words

\[
s_0=(),\qquad s_{m+1}=(s_m,1+qc_m,s_m).
\]

Direct induction gives length (2^m-1), mass (c_m-1), and, for (m\ge1),

\[
\prod_{h\in s_m}(1-h)=-1/c_m.
\tag{12}
\]

Take both child histories to be nonzero one-dimensional unit-curvature quadratic trajectories on (s_m), use the bridge (1+qc_m), and paste by (3)--(5). Their terminal quadratic ratios are (1/c_m^2). For any parameters satisfying (3), the clock-only terminal ratio is

\[
\frac{2r(d-\theta)-(2\tau+1)r^2}{(c_m+\theta)^2}
\le\frac{(d-\theta)^2}{(2\tau+1)(c_m+\theta)^2}
\le\frac{q^2}{2c_m-1}.
\tag{13}
\]

The full ratio in (11) is a weighted average of this ratio, the suffix quadratic ratio (1/c_m^2), and a zero contribution from the frozen prefix coordinate. Therefore the actual normalized error (E_m) produced by every such feasible pasted instance satisfies

\[
E_m\le\max\{c_m^{-2},q^2/(2c_m-1)\}.
\tag{14}
\]

For \(r>0\), these pasted functions have a unique minimizer in their three-dimensional space. Indeed \(\arg\min F_K=K^\circ\), since \(\nabla F_K=P_K\). Each nonzero Silver quadratic child contains positive and negative scalar gradients. The prefix inequalities for \(z\in K^\circ\) force \(z_{\rm clock}\le0\); the suffix inequalities force \(z_{\rm clock}\ge0\). Then both scalar child components must vanish. Thus \(K^\circ=\{0\}\), and changing the selected minimizer cannot improve (14). If \(r=0\), the polar cone instead forces \(z_B=0\), \(z_{\rm clock}\le0\). The initial prefix gradient gives \(u_0z_A+pz_{\rm clock}\le0\), so \(\langle X_0,z\rangle\le(T-p)z_{\rm clock}\le0\). Hence 0 is still the closest minimizer and the denominator conclusion remains valid.

For comparison with the exact benchmark, (G(x,y)\le4x+y), as (\sqrt{y^2+8xy}\le y+4x). Combining this with the source bound (C_n\le(n+1)^\rho), induction over the exact integer-split recurrence gives

\[
B_n\le4(n+1)^\rho.
\tag{15}
\]

In that induction use (a^\rho+b^\rho\le(a+b)^\rho), with (a=k+1,b=l+1). At (n_m=2^{m+1}-1), ((n_m+1)^\rho=\lambda c_m); (14)--(15) imply

\[
\limsup_{m\to\infty} B_{n_m}E_m
\le 2\lambda q^2=2(\sqrt2-1)<1.
\tag{16}
\]

This is an all-depth failure of the displayed pasted witnesses. It is not an upper bound on (W_{n_m}(s_{m+1})), and it does not exclude a proof that chooses a global Huber or quadratic witness instead. In particular, those alternative witnesses must not be discarded when interpreting (16).

The responsible cross row can also be seen without the final ratio. Choose the first child's terminal (a>0), so its start is (-c_ma). Its post-bridge frozen coordinate is (-da). Because the second child has zero quadratic residual, (7), applied to the old start and the suffix terminal, requires

\[
[d-\theta-c_mr](1+r)p^2\ge dc_ma^2.
\]

Since (r\le d/c_m=q), necessarily (p^2\ge c_ma^2/(1+q)), and (T^2\ge c_m^3a^2/(1+q)). The old prefix radius was only (c_m^2a^2). This explains why making the auxiliary coordinate larger eventually replaces the child information by a single-cut clock witness.

## 4. Line 2: shared evolving coordinates

### 4.1 Exact pasting without any extra initial coordinate

Suppose a projection-type prefix ends at (u_k=a_k=a\ne0). A bridge (\alpha=1+d) sends it to (-da). Embed a suffix history by an isometry and a common scaling so that its start is exactly (-da), and take the convex hull of the two sets of gradients and 0 in their shared space. This produces an actual global function (1) if and only if its cross projection rows hold. If they hold, there is no added original radius, and the terminal ratio is the suffix ratio multiplied by (d^2\|a\|^2/\|u_0\|^2).

This was attacked at the actual histories rather than declared as a gluing lemma. A unit-curvature quadratic prefix exposes the obstruction. If its queried jets include opposite points on a line and satisfy \(g_i=x_i\), \(f_i=\|x_i\|^2/2\), then every convex 1-smooth extension agreeing with those jets is forced to be the same quadratic on the segment between them. To see this, \(H(x)=\|x\|^2/2-f(x)\) is convex for convex 1-smooth \(f\); at both endpoints \(H=0\) and \(\nabla H=0\). Convexity forces \(H=0\), hence \(\nabla f(x)=x\), on the entire segment.

For (m\ge2), a quadratic prefix on (s_m), started at (-c_ma), has queried points (-c_ma) and (qc_ma>0), and terminal (a). The next bridge (1+qc_m) lands at (-qc_ma), inside that already forced quadratic segment. A following unit step reaches the minimizer 0 exactly and all later steps stay there. Adding dimensions with zero initial components and keeping these old jets cannot change that fact. Thus a recursive rule cannot extend this already chosen near-extremal prefix history through an arbitrary suffix at no cost.

This is a failure of a universal history-preserving child-extension claim. The original adversary may choose a different prefix history after seeing the entire word, so it does not disprove the target.

### 4.2 A realizable transverse perturbation, with its exact budget

To test whether a small curvature loss repairs that obstruction, let (0<\kappa<1) and let (u_0,\ldots,u_k\) be the GD history of the quadratic (\kappa\|u\|^2/2) on the prefix. Embed it with a new coordinate initially 0. Retain all old jets for (i<k), and replace only the terminal gradient by

\[
x_k=(u_k,0),\qquad
g_k=(\kappa u_k,\eta),\qquad
f_k=\kappa\|u_k\|^2/2.
\tag{17}
\]

The prefix GD positions are unchanged, because this terminal gradient has not yet been used. For every earlier jet, its two directed interpolation slacks against (17) are both

\[
\frac{\kappa(1-\kappa)}2\|u_k-u_i\|^2-\frac{\eta^2}2.
\]

Both directed minimizer slacks are

\[
\frac{\kappa(1-\kappa)}2\|u_k\|^2-\frac{\eta^2}2.
\]

Thus all these rows hold exactly when

\[
\eta^2\le\kappa(1-\kappa)
\min\bigl(\{\|u_k\|^2\}\cup
\{\|u_k-u_i\|^2:0\le i<k\}\bigr).
\tag{18}
\]

A repeated query \(u_i=u_k\) forces \(\eta=0\), which matters for unrestricted words including zero steps.

For completeness, these consistent finite jets have a concrete static realization. Include the minimizer jet ((0,0,0)), and, over all prescribed jets, set

\[
H(g)=\frac12\|g\|^2+
\max_i\left\{\phi_i+\langle x_i-g_i,g-g_i\rangle\right\},
\qquad
\phi_i=\langle g_i,x_i\rangle-f_i-\tfrac12\|g_i\|^2,
\qquad F=H^*.
\tag{19}
\]

The interpolation inequalities say that the (i)-th affine term is active at (g_i); hence (x_i\in\partial H(g_i)). The quadratic term makes (H) 1-strongly convex, so (F) is finite, differentiable and 1-smooth with the specified jets. The included 0 jet and its inequalities give (H(0)=0), (0\in\partial H(0)); thus (F(0)=0) is an attained minimum. Formula (19) realizes (17) before the bridge, without a function change afterwards.

The scale in (18) is the terminal distance, not the original radius. If a proposed prefix has \(\|u_k\|\le K R/C\), its bridge obeys \(\alpha\le A C\), and \(R=\|u_0\|\), then

\[
\alpha|\eta|\le AK R\sqrt{\kappa(1-\kappa)}.
\tag{20}
\]

For (1-\kappa=O(C^{-2})), this is only (O(R/C)) transverse displacement. In particular, the absolute statement “gradient of size (1/\alpha) costs curvature (1/\alpha^2)” cannot be applied to a normalized near-extremal prefix whose terminal radius is itself of order (R/C). No conclusion about the full future objective is inferred from (20) alone.

### 4.3 Changing the whole shared history

A two-mode quadratic provides a literal shared-history replacement, not an endpoint-only injection. For orthogonal eigenvectors

\[
v_1=(\cos\theta,\sin\theta),\qquad
v_2=(\sin\theta,-\cos\theta),
\]

curvatures \(\lambda_1,\lambda_2\in(0,1]\), initial point \(e_1\), and

\[
P_t(z)=\prod_{i<t}(1-h_i z),
\]

the actual quadratic trajectory has transverse coordinate

\[
y_t=\sin\theta\cos\theta[P_t(\lambda_1)-P_t(\lambda_2)].
\]

If (P_k(\lambda_1)=P_k(\lambda_2)=P\ne0), then (y_k=0), but its transverse gradient is

\[
\sin\theta\cos\theta P(\lambda_1-\lambda_2).
\]

All past values, gradients and the initial radius now belong to a single actual quadratic; (18) does not apply because the whole history changed. Keeping this quadratic for the whole word, however, gives exactly

\[
2f(x_n)=\cos^2\theta\,\lambda_1P_n(\lambda_1)^2
+\sin^2\theta\,\lambda_2P_n(\lambda_2)^2,
\]

a convex combination of scalar quadratic errors. Rotation alone therefore supplies no stronger witness than the scalar quadratic family. A nonquadratic suffix is still necessary for this proposed repair to do additional work.

One direct attempted static extension is

\[
F(X)=\sup_{g\in K}\{\langle X,g\rangle-\tfrac12g^T A^{-1}g\},
\tag{21}
\]

where \(A\) has the two curvatures and \(K\) contains every old quadratic gradient and 0. All old quadratic jets are retained automatically, and \(0\prec A\preceq I\) makes (21) 1-smooth. This gives real freedom to cap future gradients without paying a new initial coordinate. It also exposes the exact sticking point: whenever \(AX\) lies in the old gradient hull, its unconstrained maximizer is still available, so (21) is forced to take the original quadratic gradient at \(X\). The future cap cannot simply be placed across that hull.

### 4.4 A shared-history cap that does paste exactly

There is a concrete special case of (21) where the cap can be placed and all cross rows close with no auxiliary initial radius.

Let the actual two-dimensional quadratic prefix have Hessian \(A\), \(0\prec A\preceq I\), initial point \(e_1\), and points \(x_i\) with

\[
e_2^Tx_i\ge0\quad(0\le i\le k),\qquad x_k=P e_1.
\tag{22}
\]

All these are statements about the actual prefix trajectory. Its old gradients are \(g_i=Ax_i\), its values are \(x_i^TAx_i/2\), and its original radius is 1.

Suppose the bridge \(\alpha\), suffix mass \(\tau\), and numbers \(r>0,s\ge0\) satisfy

\[
P(e_1-\alpha A e_1)
=-sA^{-1}e_2-rA^{-2}e_2,\qquad s\ge\tau r.
\tag{23}
\]

Set \(b=-rA^{-1}e_2\), and use the single fixed function (21) with

\[
K=\operatorname{conv}(\{0,b\}\cup\{Ax_i:0\le i\le k\}).
\tag{24}
\]

Each old gradient is its unconstrained quadratic maximizer in (21), so every old point, gradient and function value is unchanged. For a suffix partial mass \(t_j\), prescribe

\[
X_j=-sA^{-1}e_2-rA^{-2}e_2+t_jrA^{-1}e_2.
\]

This is exactly the GD recursion if the suffix gradient is \(b\). Its metric projection normal is

\[
q_j=X_j-A^{-1}b=-(s-t_jr)A^{-1}e_2.
\]

For every old gradient,

\[
\langle q_j,Ax_i-b\rangle
=-(s-t_jr)e_2^Tx_i
-r(s-t_jr)\|A^{-1}e_2\|^2\le0.
\tag{25}
\]

The row against 0 is also nonpositive, and the row against \(b\) is zero. Thus \(b\) is exactly the maximizing gradient throughout the suffix, including its terminal point. All old-to-new rows hold because the old metric projection normals are 0; (25) checks the reverse direction. All data come from the one function (21),(24).

It follows, with the same original start and selected minimizer 0, that this admissible full-word instance has

\[
2F(X_l)
=r^2 e_2^TA^{-3}e_2
+2r(s-\tau r)e_2^TA^{-2}e_2.
\tag{26}
\]

No child was restarted and no initial coordinate or radius was added. The steps after the bridge may be any nonnegative word with the prescribed mass \(\tau\); individual zero or large steps do not invalidate the monotonic partial-mass check.

The parameter constraints in (23) can be checked without an unproved geometric lemma. Write

\[
m_j=e_1^TA^je_1,\quad D=\det A,\quad a_{12}=e_1^TAe_2\ne0.
\]

Solving the two linear equations in (23), or using Cayley--Hamilton, gives

\[
s=\frac{P}{a_{12}}(\alpha m_3-m_2),
\qquad
r=\frac{PD}{a_{12}}(m_1-\alpha m_2).
\tag{27}
\]

When \(P/a_{12}>0\), a sufficient and, apart from endpoint degeneracies, necessary interval for \(r>0,s\ge\tau r\) is

\[
\frac{m_2+\tau Dm_1}{m_3+\tau Dm_2}
\le\alpha<\frac{m_1}{m_2}.
\tag{28}
\]

The interval is nonempty for a genuinely mixed pair of distinct positive eigenvalues because \(m_2^2<m_1m_3\). That inequality follows by Cauchy--Schwarz applied to the eigenvalue moments.

Condition (22) also has a concrete sufficient prefix test. In the rotated two-mode setup of Section 4.3, let \(0<\lambda_1<\lambda_2\le1\), \(0<\theta<\pi/2\), assume

\[
P_k(\lambda_1)=P_k(\lambda_2)=P<0,
\]

and suppose exactly one prefix step exceeds \(1/\lambda_2\). Equality and the negative product imply that this unique step exceeds \(1/\lambda_1\), and that no other factor vanishes. Before that step both products are positive and their ratio is at least 1. After it both products are negative; their ratio is nondecreasing and ends at 1, hence is at most 1. Therefore \(P_i(\lambda_1)-P_i(\lambda_2)\ge0\) for every earlier query, giving (22). Also \(a_{12}<0\), so \(P/a_{12}>0\) as required in (28).

This sufficient test has a substantial restriction: (28) forces \(\alpha< m_1/m_2\le1/\lambda_1\), whereas the unique earlier sign-changing step exceeds \(1/\lambda_1\). The selected bridge is therefore smaller than that earlier step. It cannot be applied automatically to the maximum step of an arbitrary word. A unit bridge is also excluded in the genuinely mixed case because \(m_2/m_3>1\) when the positive eigenvalues are at most 1 and are not both 1.

Equations (22)--(28) are an actual compatible-prefix/static-suffix construction. What is missing is not its interpolation: it is a proof that every word needing a stronger witness admits such a compatible prefix or a recursively compatible replacement, and that the payoff (26), with all previous history retained, yields the exact \(B_n\) bound. No such selection or uniform loss sum was obtained. This special pasting therefore does not close the original target and is not asserted to do so.

## 5. Closure audit and stopping status

The auxiliary-coordinate construction passes static realizability, finite dimension, attained minimum, GD chronology, original-radius accounting, and explicit cross-history interpolation. The all-depth ceiling (16) applies only to its stated witness family and includes a minimizer-set check. For any prescribed \(L>0\), replacing a constructed \(F\) by \(LF\) gives an \(L\)-smooth function, leaves normalized GD unchanged, and leaves \(2(f_n-f_\star)/(L\|x_0-x_\star\|^2)\) equal to the displayed ratio.

The endpoint-only replacement has an exact realizable prefix (17)--(19), but no all-word suffix construction. Changing the whole shared history by an actual two-mode quadratic leads to the exact static cap (22)--(28), preserving every old value and the original radius under its explicit conditions. Those conditions have not been proved available for arbitrary words or closed under repeated pasting.

The missing work is the mathematical content of the original problem: a choice of globally compatible histories for every word, or a different sharp universal lower argument, together with a schedule-independent (1-o(1)) loss against the exact integer-split profile. It is not a technical interpolation check and was not assumed as a lemma. No full-function asymptotic upper certificate beating (1/B_n) was produced either.

This invocation stops at its one-call/two-line allocation. The original target remains OPEN; none of these drafts is submitted or accepted as a project fact.

# Fresh direct proof attempt: close the original Zhang-Jiang profile converse

At most TWO substantive proof lines in this invocation, both aimed directly at closing the original problem; no sequence of finite toy models or parameterization-only detours. No additional agents/models or worker/service actions.

## Latest human steering, not merely a new mathematical label

The user explicitly asks for independent judgment about which ideas can close the problem, more direct attacks, fewer equivalent formulations and fixed-step toy models, and inspiration from Zhang and Jiang's Silver/concatenation proof. Parent has stopped the unallocated Green-matrix parameterization and is not renewing the old coefficient-routing portfolio. This call is the new main direction, not another variant of a fixed H_m dual.

## The actual target

For any nonnegative predetermined word h of length n, W_n(h) is the supremum of2(f_n-fstar)/(L||x0-xstar||^2) over ALL finite-dimensional convex L-smooth functions with an attained minimum, every selected minimizer and original start. W_n^star=inf_(h>=0)W_n(h). W=2R.

Let C0=B0=1, Omega(x,y)=(x+y+sqrt(x^2+6xy+y^2))/2 and G(x,y)=2x+y/2+sqrt(y^2+8xy)/2. For ALL integer splits k+l=n-1, C_n=max Omega(C_k,C_l), B_n=max G(C_k,B_l), and exact U_n^ZJ=1/B_n. The affirmative goal is

    for every epsilon>0, exists N_epsilon independent of h,
    for every n>=N_epsilon and every h in[0,infinity)^n,
    W_n(h)>=(1-epsilon)/B_n.

Zero steps, very large steps, nonrecursive words, original last iterate and original radius are mandatory. Do not replace exact B_n by0.4239 or merely its exponent. A rigorous full-class asymptotic separation would instead be an acceptable negative answer, if actually obtained. Do not assume which answer is true.

## Concrete primary source and direct starting calculation

Read Zhang and Jiang's actual v2 proof https://arxiv.org/html/2410.12395v2, Sections2.2,3.1,3.2, especially Lemma15 and the retained square/composition identities. Primary-source access is allowed; do not search for a posted solution to this exact benchmark.

Those local calculations are KNOWN-TYPE calibration only; rederiving them or optimizing a one-cut lower family is not the deliverable.

Primary intended proof line: turn the primitive/dominant equality geometry into an ACTUAL recursive hard-function/interpolation construction or sharp splitting argument for arbitrary words. Work on the construction itself. Keep the old function values, gradients, cross-history interpolation rows, minimizer and normalized radius when joining children. One static function is chosen after the whole word is known and before running it; the function cannot be reselected after a pivot. Direct sums do not freeze inactive child coordinates automatically. A geometric lift may not hide radius or value losses.

If a typed recursive adversary works, prove the schedule decomposition and sum every accumulated loss to give1-o(1), UNIFORM in the whole word, with the exact C_n/B_n recurrences over all integer splits. A proved exact finite-n converse would be stronger, but do not assume the unproved completeness of primitive/dominant schedules or rank-one certificates. The source's sufficient construction is not a necessary characterization of arbitrary schedules.

An optional second proof line may use the full retained inequalities of the same source to produce a genuine complete negative construction; it must culminate in an explicit all-depth full-function certificate and a strict benchmark separation, not another conditional transport identity. Do not drift back to the closed fixed-fan/source models in the R3 reports.

## What counts in this call

Aim for a complete proof of the actual target. Reason directly and deeply; use finite examples only as internal base checks required by an actual general argument, not as the final output. New equivalences, generic Markov/Green/PEP formulations, necessary-cut catalogues, solver patterns and bounded witness-family exclusions are not substitutes for closure.

If a putative closure requires a new lemma, try to prove it and independently attack the proof yourself against realizability, uniform constants, all-horizon quantifiers and inherited histories. The positive root of X²-(A+B)X-AB is Omega(A,B), proving the join inequality and strictness. The join is increasing in both entries. Induction in the exact primitive DP gives C_n<=(n+1)^p. Conversely balanced joins recursively give equality at n=2^j-1, including j=0. This proves the equality characterization. Source check: the downloaded arXiv2410.12395v2 text Proposition4.8 states the upper bound and dyadic equality; its AppendixA.1 supplies a different calculus proof of the equivalent inequality (1+t^p)(1+(1-t)^p)<=2. The calculation here is self-contained and remains unverified in the project graph. It only concerns the exact benchmark DP.", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"fact_ids": ["b98bb807d514e80d", "0be8b54e0a33381c"], "source": "arXiv:2410.12395v2 Proposition4.8 and AppendixA.1, direct text checked", "scope": "exact DP only; supports seed scoring; no new verification submission", "status": "unverified direct calculation, known inequality independently recovered"}, "glossary": {"rho": "Project silver exponent log_2(1+sqrt2).", "C_n": "Shifted primitive DP value S_circle(n)+1.", "Omega": "Shifted primitive join (A+B+sqrt((A+B)^2+4AB))/2.", "F_p": "Auxiliary scalar function (1+t)^p-1, local to this derivation.", "D": "The proposed power-norm upper bound for the shifted join."}}
{"id": "7e14f9c47de04c17", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T11:20:26.700434+00:00", "author": "max3", "kind": "conclusion", "claim": "New scalar mechanism: for a fixed odd monotone 1-Lipschitz derivative, GD sends the entire initial interval to an exactly computable centered interval. The recursion retains arbitrary inner returns and yields a legal single-initial-point witness with full area and normalization. A complete unit-curvature symmetric-Huber subfamily bound on H_m is proved as a check; the optimized full scalar profile comparison remains open.", "evidence": "# Exact interval reachability for odd scalar gradients\n\nStatus: unverified construction and supporting calculations. No further verifier\nsubmission is allocated. This is not the arbitrary-schedule profile theorem.\n\n## 1. A legal scalar construction with an exact one-state recursion\n\nLet g:[0,infinity)->[0,infinity) be nondecreasing, 1-Lipschitz, and g(0)=0.\nExtend it oddly to the real line and define f(x)=integral_0^|x| g(r) dr.\nThe odd extension is nondecreasing and 1-Lipschitz: for opposite signs use\ng(r)+g(s)<=r+s. Thus f is convex, differentiable, 1-smooth, even, and minimized\nat zero. It is fixed before the run.\n\nFor each nonnegative step h define\n\n    M_h(R)=max_{0<=r<=R} |r-h g(r)|,       R>=0.\n\nFor a prescribed schedule h_0,...,h_(n-1), set\n\n    R_(t+1)=M_(h_t)(R_t),                R_0>0.                 (1)\n\nThe set of all points reachable after t steps from the entire initial interval\n[-R_0,R_0] is exactly [-R_t,R_t]. Indeed x->x-h g(x) is continuous and odd.\nThe image of a compact interval under a continuous real function is an interval;\noddness makes this interval symmetric, and its radius is the displayed maximum.\nApply this observation inductively. In particular some single initial point\nx_0 in [-R_0,R_0] produces terminal point R_n. The point can be chosen after\nthe complete prescribed schedule is known, by successively choosing preimages.\nIt is not changed during the trajectory. If R_n>0, that initial point is nonzero.\n\nConsequently\n\n    W_n(h) >= 2 integral_0^R_n g(r) dr / R_0^2.                (2)\n\nThe possibly smaller actual initial distance only strengthens (2). No change of\nnormalization and no random/adaptive step choice is involved.\n\nTaking the supremum in (2) over R_0>0 gives exactly the worst-case normalized\ngap for this fixed f over all nonzero scalar initial points. One direction was\njust proved. Conversely take R_0=|x_0| for any prescribed initial point; its\nterminal absolute value is <=R_n and f is nondecreasing on the positive ray.\nTaking also the supremum over g therefore gives exactly the full even-function\nscalar adversary class, rather than merely a subfamily of its trajectories.\nThis equality does not identify its value or compare it to the project target.\n\nFor a finite piecewise-linear g, every maximum in M_h is attained at R, zero,\nor one of the gradient knots in [0,R], because r-hg(r) is affine between knots.\nThe retained terminal area is a sum of trapezoids plus the final partial piece.\nThis keeps a globally fixed legal derivative and all its areas. It permits\narbitrary repeated inner returns. The scalar state is exact here because it\nrepresents a whole reachable interval, not a single point with forgotten data.\n\n## 2. Symmetric Huber interval maps\n\nFor g(r)=min(r,1), direct maximization gives\n\n    M_h(R)=|1-h|R                         if 0<=R<=1,\n    M_h(R)=R-h                            if R>=1 and 0<=h<=1,\n    M_h(R)=max(R-h,h-1)                   if R>=1 and h>=1.    (3)\n\nAll formulas agree at their common endpoints. M_h is continuous and\nnondecreasing as a function of R. For h>=1, the equivalent formula is\n\n    M_h(R)=max(R-h,(h-1)min(R,1)).\n\nWrite E(R)=R^2 for R<=1 and E(R)=2R-1 for R>=1. For a Huber derivative with\ncore curvature lambda in (0,1] and threshold radius r>0, the substitution x=ry\ngives the unit-core maps (3) with steps lambda h. Its objective normalization\nfrom initial interval radius rR is lambda E(R_n)/R^2. Thus optimizing the\nthreshold and the initial point can be done through the exact interval maps;\ntesting only one initial point of a fixed Huber function does not do this.\n\n## 3. An exact full-class bound for the unit-curvature Huber subfamily\n\nPut c=13/5, a=c^(-17), b_j=a c^j, and H_0=(), H_(m+1)=(H_m,b_m,H_m).\nLet s_m=sum H_m=a(c^m-2^m)/(c-2). Write F_m for composition of the maps (3)\nin the order H_m. Every threshold radius is allowed in this section, but the\ncore curvature is exactly one.\n\nAll steps in H_17 are less than one. Starting from R>=0, its interval radius\nis nonincreasing. If its final radius is greater than one, every radius during\nthis block was greater than one, so F_17(R)=R-s_17. Conversely if R>s_17+1,\neach partial sum is <=s_17, so all radii are >1 and the same formula holds.\nTherefore, since M_1(R)=(R-1)_+,\n\n    M_1(F_17(R))=(R-s_17-1)_+.\n\nThe following H_17 is radius-nonincreasing. Hence\n\n    F_18(R) <= (R-v_18)_+,     v_18=1+s_17.                   (4)\n\nFor d=2/c=10/13, d^3<1/2 and d^2<4/5 imply d^17<1/40. Therefore\ns_17/b_17=(1-d^17)/(c-2)>=13/8, and b_17=1. Thus v_18>=21/8>c=b_18.\n\nSuppose F_m(R)<=(R-v_m)_+ and v_m>=b_m. For b>=1, equation (3) gives\nM_b(y)<=max(y-b,b-1). Monotonicity and the inductive bound yield\n\n    F_(m+1)(R)\n      <= (M_(b_m)((R-v_m)_+)-v_m)_+\n      <= (R-2v_m-b_m)_+,\n\nbecause b_m-1-v_m<=0. Set v_(m+1)=2v_m+b_m. Since c<3,\nv_(m+1)>=3b_m>=c b_m=b_(m+1), so the induction holds for all m>=18.\nExplicitly v_m=s_m-2^(m-18)s_17.\n\nFor a unit-curvature symmetric Huber function with arbitrary threshold and\ninitial point, E(F_m(R))<=2(R-v_m)_+. Consequently\n\n    sup_(R>0) E(F_m(R))/R^2 <= 1/(2v_m) <= 1/(2a c^m).       (5)\n\nThe scalar maximization uses max_(R>=v) 2(R-v)/R^2=1/(2v), attained at R=2v.\nThus all repeated-return trajectories of every unit-curvature symmetric Huber\nfunction are controlled on H_m. This is not a bound for all core curvatures,\nall scalar convex functions, or the unrestricted worst case.\n\n## 4. A sharper quadratic-prefix calculation\n\nThe same H_m has every scalar quadratic prefix product bounded in absolute\nvalue by one, uniformly in the curvature lambda in [0,1]. This strengthens an\nearlier coarse bound of 27 in the recursive-family package, but is not used as\nan accepted fact.\n\nFor lambda>0 choose k>=17 and y in (1/c,1] so that y=a lambda c^k. Let\nP_m=product_(j=0)^(m-1)(1-a lambda c^j)^(2^(m-j-1)) and\nR_u=P_u(1-a lambda c^u). Each ordered prefix is a product of R_u indexed by\nthe nonzero binary digits of its length. It suffices to bound every |R_u| by1.\nFor u<=k all relevant steps times lambda are <=1, so the assertion follows.\n\nFor u=k+r, r>=1, first bound whole blocks. Unless a factor is zero, put\nd=2/c. Summing log(1-z)<=-z for negative scales and\nlog|1-c^j y|<=j log c+log y for positive scales gives\n\n    log|P_u|/2^(u-k-1)\n       <= -2y(1-d^k)/(c-2)+log[y(1-y)]+2log c\n       <= -5/4+log(169/100)\n       <= -14/25 < -1/2.\n\nThus |R_(k+r)|<=c^r exp(-2^(r-2)). For r>=4 this is <=1, since c<e and\n2^(r-2)>=r. (The latter inequality holds at r=4 and is preserved by doubling.)\n\nFor r=1,2,3 put A=P_(k+1). Then |A|<=1-y, and all c^j y-1 for j>=1 are\nnonnegative. Define L_j=(c^j-1)^2/(4c^j), the maximum of\n(1-y)(c^j y-1) over real y. We have\n\n    |R_(k+1)| <= L_1,\n    |R_(k+2)| <= L_1 L_2,\n    |R_(k+3)| <= L_1^2 L_2 L_3.\n\nHere L_1=16/65<1/4, L_2=5184/4225<5/4, and\nL_3=1073296/274625<4. The displayed three bounds are respectively <1/4,\n<5/16, and <5/16. This proves the claim. Cases containing a zero factor are\nimmediate. Lambda=0 also gives prefix products equal to1.\n\nIn particular, for the unit-core maps with scaled steps lambda H_m, the entire\ninitial interval [-1,1] remains in the core during H_m, and its final radius is\n|P_m(lambda)|. This justifies exact whole-block skipping in tiny interval-map\nchecks; it is not a computational proof of the all-curvature Huber bound.\n\n## 5. Current boundary and failed simplifications\n\nFor arbitrary lambda, choose k so all steps of lambda H_k are <=1. Its radius\nmap obeys F_k(R)<=max(1,R-lambda s_k). If lambda s_j>=lambda b_j-2 at subsequent\nlevels, that inequality propagates with the exact sums: apply (3) between the\ntwo copies and absorb the overshoot peak lambda b_j-1 into lambda s_j+1.\nThis bounds large input radii but leaves the small-radius objective ratio open.\n\nA proposed two-line majorant max(qR,R-v) was not closed at full sum capacity.\nThe internal peak term q(b-1)min(F(R),1) survives composition; dropping it\nincorrectly suggests faster contraction. In particular, at the input where\nthe first block reaches radius1 it produces q(b-1), and any new linear bound\nmust dominate that peak. This is the precise gap in the attempted simple\nradius-potential induction, not an all-scalar stability theorem.\n\nTiny exploratory checks used three curvatures (.5,.75,.9), three initial radii\n(10,100,10^6), and four depths (20,26,34,44). Whole-core and whole-outside blocks\nwere evaluated by their formulas; the largest call count was155, and times\nwere below .001 seconds per case. Logarithmic radii avoided underflow. Every\nfixed tested interval eventually contracted very sharply. These 36 values\ngive neither a uniform-in-curvature nor a uniform-in-radius theorem.\n\nThe next construction problem is to choose a fixed multi-knot monotone gradient\nfor (1) with a quantitatively large terminal area. The interval formulation\nremoves chronology from the reachable-state description, but it does not remove\nthe global monotonicity/Lipschitz restrictions on the derivative. No comparison\nof its optimized value with the exact ZJ benchmark has been proved.\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"artifact": "research/max3_scalar_interval_reachability.md", "status": "unverified; no additional verifier call", "mechanism": "exact interval images for a fixed even scalar function", "fact_ids": []}, "glossary": {"M_h": "Exact radius of the image of [-R,R] under x -> x-h g(x), namely max_{0<=r<=R}|r-hg(r)|.", "R_t": "Radius of the entire interval reachable at time t from a prescribed centered initial interval.", "F_m": "Composition of the scalar radius maps in the recursive schedule H_m; local to this candidate.", "v_m": "Unit-core Huber extinction threshold lower bound, v_18=1+s_17 and v_(m+1)=2v_m+b_m.", "E": "Twice the unit-threshold Huber value: E(R)=R^2 for R<=1 and 2R-1 for R>=1."}}
{"id": "4a53ae21f8dddf30", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T12:17:55.037141+00:00", "author": "max3", "kind": "conclusion", "claim": "C11 scoped obstruction now covers arbitrarily many nested inward scalar returns on H_m, including asymmetric functions, arbitrary curvature, and nonconstant ordinary blocks: normalized terminal objective <=(42/13)/(a c^(m-1)), hence its exact-profile ratio tends to zero. Adding inward knots cannot fix this channel; a future scalar witness must genuinely revisit or extend a previously traversed ray interval.", "evidence": "# Arbitrarily many nested inward scalar returns remain too weak on H_m\n\nStatus: complete scoped candidate proofs, not verifier-accepted. No additional\nverification call is allocated. The main result allows asymmetric functions,\narbitrary core curvature, nonconstant gradients between crossings, and any\nnumber of crossings. Its essential restriction is the nested-return condition\ndefined below. It is not an upper bound on the full scalar or multidimensional\nworst case.\n\n## 1. A schedule-level obstruction for nested returns\n\nLet f:R->R be differentiable and convex, with 1-Lipschitz derivative g,\nf(0)=0=min f, and let x_(t+1)=x_t-h_t g(x_t) for a finite nonnegative schedule.\nWrite H=max_t h_t and S=sum_t h_t. Assume that some eta>=1 has the property\n\n    sum_(t<j) h_t >= eta h_j and sum_(t>j) h_t >= eta h_j\n    whenever h_j>1.                                             (1)\n\nA nonzero terminal objective implies that the trajectory never hits a\nminimizer. Reflect the line if needed so that x_0>0. List every strict crossing\nof zero as checkpoint steps b_0,...,b_(k-1), and let block_i be the intervening\nsame-sign steps, with sum s_i. Let a_i be the absolute position at the start of\nblock_i, and r_i its absolute position at its end, before the next checkpoint;\nset r_k=|x_n|. Thus a_0=x_0. Each block has nonincreasing absolute positions.\n\nThe nested-return hypothesis is exactly\n\n    a_(i+2) <= r_i,                  0<=i<=k-2.                  (2)\n\nEach re-entry to a ray therefore lands inside the interval traversed on its\nprevious visit. There is no assumption that the derivative is constant on a\nblock, that the two rays agree, or that their curvature is one.\n\nFor k>=1, conditions (1)-(2) imply\n\n    2 f(x_n)/x_0^2 <= 2(1+1/eta)/H.                            (3)\n\nFor k=0, the sharper bound\n\n    2 f(x_n)/x_0^2 <= 1/(1+2S)                                (4)\n\nholds without (1). If the terminal objective is zero all these bounds hold\ndirectly. Scaling f by L gives the same normalized statements for arbitrary\nL>0 and steps h_t/L.\n\n### Proof\n\nOn either ray, put G(r)=|g(plus-or-minus r)|. Convexity and smoothness give\n0<=G(r)<=r and make G nondecreasing and 1-Lipschitz. A strict sign crossing\nrequires its step b>1. Let u be the positive gradient magnitude immediately\nbefore the first crossing. The preceding block gives\n\n    a_0 >= (1+s_0)u,\n    a_1 = b_0 u-r_0 <= (b_0-1)u.\n\nThe first inequality follows since every earlier gradient magnitude is at\nleast u and r_0>=u. Since s_0 is the entire prefix before b_0, (1) gives\n\n    a_1 <= [(b_0-1)/(1+s_0)]a_0 <= a_0/eta.                    (5)\n\nBy (2), every later point on the initial ray has radius <=a_0, and every\npoint on the opposite ray has radius <=a_1. This controls all iterates in the\nfixed interval [-a_1,a_0], hence every single-step displacement by a_0+a_1.\n\nLet w=|g(x_n)|>0 and let v be the gradient magnitude immediately before the\nlast crossing b=b_(k-1). All tail gradients are at least w, so the landing\nradius on the final ray satisfies\n\n    a_k >= (1+s_k)w.\n\nHere r_k>=w accounts for the extra 1. The crossing itself gives\n\n    a_k=bv-r_(k-1)<=(b-1)v.\n\nThe entire tail after this last crossing has sum s_k, and (1) therefore yields\n\n    v >= [(1+s_k)/(b-1)]w >= eta w >= w.                       (6)\n\nNested returns and raywise monotonicity imply that every earlier gradient on\nthe final ray is at least w, while every earlier gradient on the other ray is\nat least v. Thus every sampled gradient magnitude, including at the largest\nstep H, is at least w. At that step, (5) gives\n\n    H w <= H |g(x_t)|=|x_(t+1)-x_t|\n        <= a_0+a_1 <= (1+1/eta)a_0.                           (7)\n\nFinally convexity gives f(x_n)<=|x_n|w<=a_0 w. Combining this with (7) proves\n(3). Every value and gradient belongs to the same fixed function; no oracle\nis chosen independently at different times.\n\nFor k=0, put r=|x_n| and w=|g(x_n)|. Then r+S w<=a_0. Since\nG(u)<=min(u,w) for 0<=u<=r, integration gives f(x_n)<=rw-w^2/2. Consequently\n\n    2f(x_n)<=2a_0 w-(2S+1)w^2<=a_0^2/(2S+1),\n\nwhich proves (4). This also supplies a complete area calculation for that case.\n\n## 2. Application to the recursive hostile schedules\n\nDefine c=13/5, a=c^(-17), b_u=a c^u, H_0=(), and\n\n    H_(m+1)=(H_m,b_m,H_m),\n    N_m=2^m-1,\n    s_m=sum H_m=a(c^m-2^m)/(c-2).\n\nPut eta=13/8. Since (2/c)^17<1/40, we have\n\n    s_u/b_u=[1-(2/c)^u]/(c-2)>=eta,       u>=17.               (8)\n\nEvery step greater than 1 in H_m is b_u for u>=18. Its occurrence has a\ncomplete H_u immediately before it and a complete H_u immediately after it,\nby the defining recursion. Thus its entire prefix and tail satisfy (1).\nThe largest step is B=b_(m-1). For m>=19, (3)-(4) give the uniform bound\n\n    sup_(nested scalar trajectories) 2f(x_(N_m))/|x_0|^2\n       <= (42/13)/B = [42/(5a)]c^(-m).                       (9)\n\nIn the zero-crossing case use S>=B in (4), which is smaller than (9).\nThe supremum permits any asymmetric convex 1-smooth function and any initial\npoint whose full trajectory satisfies (2). The function and all its knots may\ndepend on the full schedule and on m; there is no fixed-function-across-horizons\nrestriction.\n\nFor completeness, the elementary exact-DP bound needed to compare (9) with\nthe target profile is proved here. With C_n=S_circle(n)+1 and\nD_n=2S_bullet(n)+1, the shifted joins are\n\n    Omega(A,B)=(A+B+sqrt((A+B)^2+4AB))/2,\n    G(A,D)=2A+D/2+sqrt(D^2+8AD)/2.\n\nOne has Omega(A,B)<=(A^(3/4)+B^(3/4))^(4/3). After normalization and squaring\nat the positive root, this is equivalent to\n\n    t^(4/3)+(1-t)^(4/3)+[t(1-t)]^(4/3)<=1,  0<=t<=1.\n\nSet x=t^(1/3), y=(1-t)^(1/3). The difference from 1 is\n\n    x^3 y^3 [1/(1+x+x^2)+1/(1+y+y^2)-xy],\n\nwhich is nonnegative because the reciprocal terms are each at least 1/3\nand xy<=2^(-2/3)<2/3. Induction in the exact primitive DP gives\nC_n<=(n+1)^(4/3). Also G(A,D)<=D+4A. The dominant recursion then gives\nD_n<=1+4n^(4/3), using u^(4/3)+v^(4/3)<=(u+v)^(4/3).\n\nSince c^3>16 and U_n^ZJ=1/D_n, (9) implies\n\n    sup_(nested scalar trajectories) [2f(x_(N_m))/|x_0|^2]\n             /U_(N_m)^ZJ ->0.                               (10)\n\nThis excludes arbitrarily many nested inward returns, not mixed returns\nthat revisit or extend a previously traversed radial interval. The shared\ncoupled-coordinate candidate d1d7cfb25e10bfc5 supplies a divergent lower\nbound on the actual multidimensional W_(N_m)(H_m); there is no contradiction.\nThat separate candidate is not needed in the proof of (9)-(10).\n\n## 3. An explicit fixed multi-knot family inside this obstruction\n\nThis construction records the proposed new inward mechanism and its complete\nold-area terms. Split any nonnegative schedule into k checkpoints b_i>1 and\nk+1 ordinary blocks of sums s_i. Choose e>=0, put d_i=s_i for i<k and\nd_k=s_k+e, choose m_k>0, and define backwards\n\n    m_i=[m_(i+1)+sum_(j=i+1)^k d_j m_j]/(b_i-1),\n                                            i=k-1,...,0.       (11)\n\nRequire m_i>=m_(i+2) for 0<=i<=k-2. Put\n\n    O_i=sum_(j>i, j congruent i modulo 2) d_j m_j,\n    ell_i=m_i+O_i,     a_i=ell_i+d_i m_i.                     (12)\n\nOn the ray of parity i, prescribe a plateau of gradient magnitude m_i on\n[ell_i,a_i]. Build each ray from its innermost plateau outward. Start with a\nunit-curvature core up to its innermost m_i. Consecutive plateaus on this ray\nare connected by a slope-one ramp, since\n\n    ell_i-a_(i+2)=m_i-m_(i+2)>=0.                            (13)\n\nEqual heights simply merge. Extend the outermost plateau to infinity; if\none ray has no prescribed plateau, a unit quadratic on that ray suffices.\nThese two profiles, with the negative ray assigned a negative derivative,\ndefine one continuous, nondecreasing, globally 1-Lipschitz derivative.\nDefine f(x) by integrating it from zero. Then f is convex and 1-smooth,\nwith its fixed minimum f(0)=0.\n\nStart at x_0=a_0. For i<k, the ordinary block moves from radius a_i to ell_i\nwith gradient magnitude m_i, regardless of its individual nonnegative steps.\nThe next checkpoint lands at radius a_(i+1), because (11)-(12) give\n\n    b_i m_i-ell_i=a_(i+1).\n\nThe last ordinary block moves from (1+s_k+e)m_k to (1+e)m_k. Thus the exact\nnormalized terminal objective is\n\n    J=(1+2e)m_k^2/a_0^2.                                    (14)\n\nEquation (13) makes every return nested in the sense (2). Therefore all\nchoices of k, checkpoints, m_k, and feasible e in this construction on H_m\nare already covered by the uniform bound (9). Increasing the knot count in\nthis particular construction cannot repair the missing profile comparison.\n\nThe retained old-area defect on plateau i is\n\n    Q_i=f(x)-xg(x)+g(x)^2/2\n       =-sum_(j>i, j congruent i modulo 2)\n                      d_j m_j(m_i-m_j) =:-D_i.              (15)\n\nTo check (15), at the inner end ell_i=m_i+sum_j d_j m_j integrate the ray's\ngradient magnitude over [0,ell_i]. All unit ramps together span gradient\nmagnitudes from 0 to m_i and contribute m_i^2/2 in gradient coordinates.\nEvery inner plateau j contributes its additional area d_j m_j^2. Thus\nf(plus-or-minus ell_i)=m_i^2/2+sum_j d_j m_j^2. Subtract ell_i m_i and add\nm_i^2/2 to obtain (15). Moving along plateau i leaves f-xg unchanged, so (15)\nholds throughout.\n\nLet S be the total sum of the actual schedule, including checkpoints but\nexcluding e. For the primitive energy used in the earlier two-cut test,\n\n    V=2 sum_t h_t[f(x_t)-f(x_n)]\n       +sum_t h_t(1-h_t)g(x_t)^2-S(S+1)g(x_n)^2,\n\nthe exact formula is\n\n    V=a_0^2-(S+1+e)^2 m_k^2\n          -2 sum_(i=0)^(k-1) (s_i+b_i)D_i.                 (16)\n\nIndeed, substituting Q=f-xg+g^2/2 and telescoping\n2h xg-h^2g^2=x^2-(x-hg)^2 gives\nV=x_0^2-x_n^2-2S f(x_n)-S(S+1)m_k^2+2sum_t h_t Q_t.\nThe final plateau has Q_k=0, while |x_n|=(1+e)m_k and\nf(x_n)=(1/2+e)m_k^2.
