{"id": "1a23bf9f1a7799fd", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T12:48:25.351275+00:00", "author": "max3", "kind": "conclusion", "claim": "C13 extension removes all parameter calibration for the common-ell staircase family, while allowing arbitrary independent phases on the two rays. A balanced pair of exact flow clocks has crossing cost delta with tau+delta< s_r; summing the actual recursive word yields a uniform normalized gap <=(c^3/a)c^(-m). This covers every ell>=1, every initial point, all knot visits and crossing patterns, and horizon-dependent finite trajectory-preserving realizations; it still does not cover arbitrary scalar profiles.", "evidence": "# A full log-periodic staircase class is controlled by scalar flow time\n\nStatus: complete scoped candidate proof, not verifier-accepted. No new verifier\ncall. Unlike the earlier geometric-grid relay bound, this result puts no\nrestriction on the crossing pattern, knot visits, or gradients within ordinary\nblocks. It covers the entire explicitly specified function class below, with\nparameters and finite realizations allowed to depend on the horizon. It does\nnot cover every scalar convex smooth function or the unrestricted W_n.\n\n## 1. The recursive schedules and a legal multiscale derivative\n\nSet c=13/5, a=c^(-17), b_u=a c^u, H_0=(), and\n\n    H_(m+1)=(H_m,b_m,H_m),\n    s_u=sum H_u=a(c^u-2^u)/(c-2).\n\nFix integers m>=r>=17, and write b=b_r, S=s_r, and J=m-r. Put eta=13/8.\nSince (10/13)^3<1/2 and (10/13)^2<4/5,\n(10/13)^17<1/40. Therefore S>=eta b.\n\nChoose, independently for each ray sigma in {+,-}, a positive phase theta_sigma\nand a parameter ell_sigma satisfying\n\n    b <= ell_sigma <= 1+(S-log c)/(c-1).                     (1)\n\nThis interval is nonempty: at ell=b its associated expression\n(c-1)(b-1)+log c is smaller than S, since\neta-(c-1)=1/40 and log c<1<c-1. Define\n\n    u_sigma=c ell_sigma-(c-1),\n    M_(sigma,z)=theta_sigma c^z,          z any integer.\n\nOn ray sigma, prescribe gradient magnitude G_sigma as follows. For each z,\nlet M=M_(sigma,z) and put\n\n    G_sigma(R)=M,\n       ell_sigma M <= R <= u_sigma M;\n\n    G_sigma(R)=R-(u_sigma-1)M,\n       u_sigma M <= R <= c ell_sigma M.                     (2)\n\nThe second interval is the ramp from gradient M to cM. Its outer endpoint is\nthe next plateau's inner endpoint. These intervals cover every positive R;\ntheir endpoints agree. Set G_sigma(0)=0.\n\nThe profile is continuous, nondecreasing, and 1-Lipschitz, with slopes zero\nor one. It satisfies\n\n    G_sigma(cR)=c G_sigma(R),\n    0<=G_sigma(R)<=R/ell_sigma<=R/b.                        (3)\n\nFor the secant inequality on a ramp, write G=R-(u_sigma-1)M. The ratio G/R\nincreases to 1/ell_sigma at the ramp's outer endpoint; on a plateau the ratio\nis at most its inner-endpoint value 1/ell_sigma. Thus (3) holds everywhere.\nContinuity at zero follows from (3).\n\nDefine g(x)=G_+(x) for x>=0 and g(x)=-G_-(-x) for x<=0, and define\n\n    f(x)=integral_0^x g(t) dt.\n\nThis is one globally fixed convex differentiable 1-smooth function, with\nf(0)=0=min f. Across opposite rays, the Lipschitz inequality follows from\nG_+(R)+G_-(T)<=R+T. The phases and parameters of the two rays need not agree.\nThere is no fixed quadratic neighborhood of zero; the knots accumulate there.\n\n## 2. Exact areas and finite versions of the same trajectory\n\nWrite ell=ell_sigma and\n\n    K_sigma=1/2+(ell-1)/(c+1).\n\nAt the inner endpoint ell M of any plateau,\n\n    f(sigma ell M)=K_sigma M^2.                            (4)\n\nAll ramps below that endpoint together traverse gradient magnitudes from\nzero to M and contribute M^2/2. The lower plateaus contribute\n\n    (c-1)(ell-1)M^2 sum_(j>=1)c^(-2j)\n       =[(ell-1)/(c+1)]M^2,\n\nwhich proves (4). On the plateau itself,\n\n    f(sigma R)=K_sigma M^2+M(R-ell M).                       (5)\n\nOn the following ramp, start with (5) at R=u_sigma M and add\n[G_sigma(R)^2-M^2]/2. These are complete areas, including every inner level.\nIn particular f(sigma cR)=c^2 f(sigma R).\n\nFor any one finite nonzero trajectory, a finite-knot function can realize\nexactly the same gradients and values. On each ray choose an inner plateau\nendpoint ell M below every nonzero sampled radius. Replace the profile below\nit by a zero segment up to\n\n    delta=[c/(c+1)](ell-1)M,\n\nthen a slope-one ramp of length M, then a plateau at M up to ell M. The\nendpoint gradient remains M, and the enclosed area is\n\n    ell M^2-delta M-M^2/2=K_sigma M^2.\n\nThus all larger-radius gradients and values remain unchanged. Extend the\noutermost needed plateau to a cap beyond all sampled radii. This produces\na globally legal finite-knot function and the identical full GD trajectory,\nwith the same fixed minimizer zero and the same initial-distance normalization.\nThe number of retained levels may grow with the horizon. This is a trajectory\npreserving truncation; arbitrary unrelated core replacements are not claimed\nto preserve the bound proved below.\n\n## 3. Every ordinary block contracts by one complete radial period\n\nFor either ray, the exact flow time across one multiplicative period is\n\n    integral_(R/c)^R dt/G_sigma(t)\n        = tau_sigma :=(c-1)(ell_sigma-1)+log c.              (6)\n\nIt is independent of R. Indeed G_sigma(ct)=cG_sigma(t) makes this integral\nperiodic in log R with a full-period integration window. Evaluating from\nell_sigma M to c ell_sigma M gives plateau time u_sigma-ell_sigma and ramp\ntime log c, which proves (6). Condition (1) says tau_sigma<=S.\n\nEvery step of H_r is at most b/c. By (3), such a step h takes a nonzero\nradius R to a strictly positive smaller radius R'=R-hG_sigma(R), with\nR'>=(1-1/c)R. Therefore there are no sign crossings within any H_r block,\nregardless of its starting radius or how many knots it traverses.\n\nFor each of its steps, monotonicity gives\n\n    integral_(R')^R dt/G_sigma(t)\n        >= (R-R')/G_sigma(R)=h.                             (7)\n\nAdd (7) across the full block. If its input and output radii are A and B,\nthe flow time integral from B to A is at least S>=tau_sigma. By (6) and\npositivity of the integrand, B<=A/c. Hence every H_r, from every point on\neither ray, satisfies\n\n    |x_after H_r| <= |x_before H_r|/c.                      (8)\n\nNo endpoint is selected separately at successive steps. This is a bound\non every actual trajectory of the fixed function (2), and consequently on\nits complete interval image as well.\n\n## 4. Exact coarse-word bookkeeping and the objective bound\n\nThe exact decomposition of H_m has 2^J copies of H_r and 2^J-1 checkpoint\nsteps. A level k checkpoint has h=b c^k and occurs 2^(J-k-1) times,\nfor 0<=k<=J-1. At such a step, (3) gives\n\n    |x-hg(x)| <= max(1,h/b-1)|x| <= c^k |x|.                (9)\n\nThis allows a crossing, a same-sign step, or a zero landing. No prescribed\ncrossing pattern is used. The total checkpoint exponent is\n\n    sum_(k=0)^(J-1) k 2^(J-k-1)=2^J-J-1.\n\nCombining every copy of (8) with every copy of (9) yields\n\n    |x_(2^m-1)| <= c^(-J-1)|x_0|.                         (10)\n\nWhen J=0 the formula is just (8); the empty sum convention is consistent.\nFinally (3) implies f(x)<=x^2/(2b) by integration from the fixed minimizer.\nTherefore every initial point and every function specified by (1)-(2) obey\n\n    2f(x_(2^m-1))/|x_0|^2\n        <= b^(-1)c^(-2J-2)\n        = a^(-1)c^(-m-J-2)\n        <= [1/(a c^2)]c^(-m).                             (11)\n\nThe bound is uniform in r, both phases, both admissible ell parameters and\nthe initial point. These choices can all depend on m. The finite trajectory\npreserving versions of Section 2 have the identical objective ratio.\n\n## 5. Comparison with the exact profile and the remaining gap\n\nA self-contained rough exact-DP estimate suffices. For C_n=S_circle(n)+1\nand D_n=2S_bullet(n)+1, the shifted joins are\n\n    Omega(A,B)=(A+B+sqrt((A+B)^2+4AB))/2,\n    G(A,D)=2A+D/2+sqrt(D^2+8AD)/2.\n\nThe inequality Omega(A,B)<=(A^(3/4)+B^(3/4))^(4/3) is equivalent to\nt^(4/3)+(1-t)^(4/3)+[t(1-t)]^(4/3)<=1. To prove it put\nx=t^(1/3), y=(1-t)^(1/3). The difference from 1 is\nx^3y^3[1/(1+x+x^2)+1/(1+y+y^2)-xy]>=0, since each reciprocal is at least\n1/3 and xy<=2^(-2/3)<2/3. Induction gives C_n<=(n+1)^(4/3). The estimate\nG(A,D)<=D+4A and u^(4/3)+v^(4/3)<=(u+v)^(4/3) give D_n<=1+4n^(4/3).\nSince c^3>16, multiplying (11) by D_(2^m-1) proves that the supremum\nobjective ratio in this class, divided by U_(2^m-1)^ZJ, tends to zero.\n\nThe profile class has arbitrary numbers of knots, asymmetric ray phases,\nmultiple visits, and ordinary blocks that can traverse many knots. Its\nlimitation is instead its precise discrete homogeneity and the calibrated\nflow-time/secant range (1). Functions outside that range, nonperiodic radial\ngeometry, and the complete scalar class remain open. A scalar class bound\ndoes not upper-bound the unrestricted multidimensional W; the already shared\ncoupled-coordinate divergent example remains compatible with this result.\n\n## 6. The calibration restriction can be removed for a common staircase parameter\n\nThe following extension covers EVERY ell>=1 in the profile (2), with\nell_+=ell_-=ell, while keeping theta_+ and theta_- completely independent.\nThere is no condition (1), no prescribed crossing pattern, and no restriction\non the initial point. The number of knots, phases, ell, and the finite\ntrajectory-preserving truncations can all depend on m.\n\nFor every m>=17 this larger class satisfies the uniform bound\n\n    2f(x_(2^m-1))/|x_0|^2 <= (c^3/a)c^(-m).                (12)\n\nIt consequently also has vanishing ratio to the exact U_(2^m-1)^ZJ, by\nthe elementary benchmark estimate already proved in Section 5.\n\n### 6.1 Choose the coarse scale and define the two flow clocks\n\nChoose the smallest r>=17 for which b=b_r>=ell. Then b/c<ell<=b; the strict\nleft inequality also holds when ell=1 and r=17. Set S=s_r. Every step in H_r\nis at most b/c, and hence preserves sign by the bound |g(x)|<=|x|/ell.\n\nIf m<r, the entire H_m preserves sign. Put w=|g(x_n)| and R=|x_n|. Then\nR+s_m w<=|x_0|, while f(x_n)<=Rw-w^2/2. Completing the square gives\n\n    2f(x_n)/|x_0|^2 <=1/(1+2s_m)\n       <=[1/(2eta a)]c^(-m),\n\nwhich is smaller than (12). Hence assume m>=r and put J=m-r.\n\nReflect the function if needed so that x_0>0. Multiplying a phase theta_sigma\nby c does not change its derivative profile. We can therefore choose phase\nrepresentatives theta_+=theta and theta_-=q theta with 1<=q<c.\n\nLet G be the template (2) with plateau heights c^z and common parameter ell,\nand define its signed flow coordinate on R>0 by\n\n    F(R)=integral_ell^R dt/G(t).\n\nThe integral is negative when R<ell. Put\n\n    tau=(c-1)(ell-1)+log c,\n    E=(c-1)-log c>0,\n    F_+(R)=F(R/theta),       F_-(R)=F(R/(q theta)).\n\nThen F_sigma(cR)=F_sigma(R)+tau. Each positive or negative H_r block reduces\nits own flow clock by at least S, by the stepwise integral inequality (7).\n\n### 6.2 Exact worst crossing-clock costs\n\nDefine\n\n    D_+=F(b/q),       D_-=F(bq),\n    delta=(D_++D_-)/2,\n    v=(D_--D_+)/2,\n    P_+(R)=F_+(R),    P_-(R)=F_-(R)+v.                    (13)\n\nFor a step h=b c^k that crosses from ray sigma to the other ray, its landing\nradius is h G_sigma(R)-R<=h G_sigma(R). Its change in the unadjusted clock\nis therefore at most\n\n    k tau + F_other(b G_sigma(R))-F_sigma(R).              (14)\n\nThe final difference in (14) is maximized at an inner plateau endpoint.\nOn a plateau its first term is constant and its second increases with R,\nso the difference decreases. On a unit-slope ramp, use the current gradient\nu as coordinate; then dR/du=1 and its derivative is\n\n    b/G_other(bu)-1/u >=(ell-1)/u>=0,\n\nby the secant bound G_other(y)<=y/ell. Thus it increases on a ramp. At all\ninner plateau endpoints on one ray the difference has the same value, by\nthe common radial period and common tau. That value is exactly D_+ or D_-\nfrom (13). The additive constant v therefore makes BOTH crossing directions\nhave adjusted-clock change at most k tau+delta.\n\nThis same bound also holds for a checkpoint that does not cross. Its clock\ndecreases by at least h>=b, by (7). Since b/q>=ell/c and bq>=ell,\n\n    delta>=-tau/2>-b,\n\nwhere tau<(c-1)ell<2b. Consequently -b<=k tau+delta for every k>=0.\nA zero landing has zero terminal objective and needs no logarithmic clock.\nWe have proved, for every nonzero trajectory and every checkpoint,\n\n    change of P <= k tau+delta.                            (15)\n\n### 6.3 Uniform bound on the phase-dependent cost\n\nWe now prove\n\n    tau+delta <=(c-1)b<S.                                 (16)\n\nThis quantitative inequality is essential; it is not an assumed profile\nclosure. Put p=b/ell, so 1<=p<c. For 1<=z<=c, the template gradient is at\nleast 1 on [ell,ell z], whence\n\n    F(ell z)<=ell(z-1),       tau=(c-1)ell-E.               (17)\n\nReduce p q and p/q by powers of c into z_1,z_2 in [1,c]. Let K be the sum\nof those two powers. Then\n\n    z_1 z_2=p^2/c^K,\n    2delta<=K tau+ell(z_1+z_2-2).                          (18)\n\nFor z_1,z_2 in [1,c], their sum is at most 1+z_1z_2 when the product is\nat most c, and at most c+(z_1z_2)/c when the product is at least c.\nThese follow respectively from (z_1-1)(z_2-1)>=0 and\n(c-z_1)(c-z_2)>=0.\n\nIf 1<=p<=sqrt(c), the possible K are -1 and 0. Applying these two sum bounds\nin (18) gives\n\n    delta <=[ell(p^2-1)+E]/2.\n\nTherefore\n\n    tau+delta <=ell[p^2/2+c-3/2]-E/2 <=(c-1)b.\n\nThe last inequality is equivalent, after dropping the favorable E/2, to\n(p-1)(p-(2c-3))<=0. It holds because sqrt(c)<2c-3 for c=13/5.\n\nIf sqrt(c)<=p<c, the possible K are 0 and 1. The same calculation gives\n\n    delta <=ell[c-2+p^2/c]/2,\n    tau+delta <=ell[3c/2-2+p^2/(2c)]-E <=(c-1)b.\n\nFor the last inequality, the difference divided by ell, before the favorable\n-E term, is the convex quadratic\np^2/(2c)-(c-1)p+3c/2-2. At p=sqrt(c) it equals\n(c-1)(3/2-sqrt(c))<0; at p=c it equals -(c-1)(c-2)<0.\nConvexity therefore makes it nonpositive on the full interval. This proves\nthe first inequality in (16). The second follows from S>=eta b and\neta=13/8>c-1=8/5.\n\n### 6.4 Complete clock recurrence and normalization\n\nThere are 2^J ordinary H_r blocks, the checkpoint level sum is\n2^J-J-1, and the number of checkpoints is 2^J-1. Adding all actual clock\nchanges using (15) gives\n\n    P_final-P_initial\n       <=-2^J[S-tau-delta]-(J+1)tau-delta\n       <=-(J+1)tau-delta.                                 (19)\n\nAll crossing histories and all ray changes are included. The two clocks\ndiffer from a common reference by a controlled phase amount: since\nq in [1,c), one has F_+(R)-tau<=F_-(R)<=F_+(R), and 0<=v<=tau.\nThe latter follows because D_--D_+=F(bq)-F(b/q)<=2tau.\nThus, for either final ray, P_final>=F_+(|x_n|)-tau, whereas\nP_initial=F_+(|x_0|). Together with delta>=-tau/2, (19) yields\n\n    F_+(|x_n|)-F_+(|x_0|) <=-(J-1/2)tau\n                                  <=-(J-1)tau.\n\nThe clock is increasing and has period increment tau, so\n\n    |x_n|<=c^(1-J)|x_0|.\n\nThis bound is deliberately loose when J=0, but remains valid. The global\nsecant bound gives f(x)<=x^2/(2ell), and b/c<ell implies\n\n    2f(x_n)/|x_0|^2\n        <=ell^(-1)c^(2-2J)\n        <=b^(-1)c^(3-2J)\n        =a^(-1)c^(-m)c^(3-J)\n        <=(c^3/a)c^(-m),\n\nwhich proves (12).\n\nThe remaining restriction is genuine discrete radial homogeneity with factor\nc and a COMMON ell on both rays. Their phases are arbitrary. Section 1 also\nallows different ell values in its stated calibrated range. These results\ndo not cover arbitrary independently scaled rays outside that range,\ndifferent radial periods, nonperiodic knot geometry, or the entire scalar\nclass. The full multidimensional exact-profile target remains unresolved.\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"artifact": "research/max3_multiscale_flow_obstruction.md, Section 6", "finding_ids": ["24c3252159c7775f", "ef08dafdd85a7aae", "4a53ae21f8dddf30"], "status": "complete unverified extension; no fact_submit", "scope": "all common-ell unit-ramp staircases of radial period13/5, arbitrary ray phases; calibrated unequal-ell result retained", "next_gap": "unequal ray parameters outside the calibrated range, other periods, or nonperiodic radial geometry"}, "glossary": {"F": "Scalar template flow clock integral from ell to R of 1/G(t).", "q": "Relative ray phase, represented in [1,c) because multiplying a phase by c leaves the staircase unchanged.", "D_+, D_-": "Maximal directional crossing-clock costs F(b/q) and F(bq).", "delta": "Balanced crossing-clock cost (D_++D_-)/2.", "v": "Additive negative-ray clock shift (D_--D_+)/2.", "P_+, P_-": "Adjusted ray clocks F_+ and F_-+v.", "E": "Positive smoothing correction c-1-log c.", "p": "Ratio b/ell in [1,c), with b the smallest recursive scale at least ell."}}
{"id": "8f56dbb73920cbe5", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T12:52:40.711564+00:00", "author": "max3", "kind": "conclusion", "claim": "C14 full-class extension: EVERY even scalar convex 1-smooth profile with g((13/5)R)=(13/5)g(R), allowing arbitrary within-period shape and horizon-dependent complexity, obeys normalized GD gap <=(1/(a c))c^(-m) on H_m. The proof uses an exact flow-clock law with the proved closure tau+max_R[F(b g(R))-F(R)]<=(c-1)b<s_r. No staircase, curvature pattern, grid sampling or crossing hypothesis is required; exact-profile ratio vanishes.", "evidence": "# Arbitrary even profiles with discrete radial homogeneity are too weak on H_m\n\nStatus: complete scoped candidate proof, not verifier-accepted. No verifier\ncall. This extends the fixed-staircase analysis to every shape within a radial\nperiod. Evenness and the stated exact discrete homogeneity are essential\nhypotheses; the full scalar and multidimensional classes remain unresolved.\n\n## 1. Statement\n\nLet c=13/5, a=c^(-17), b_u=a c^u, and\n\n    H_0=(),  H_(m+1)=(H_m,b_m,H_m),\n    N_m=2^m-1,\n    s_m=sum H_m=a(c^m-2^m)/(c-2).\n\nConsider ANY nondecreasing 1-Lipschitz function g:[0,infinity)->[0,infinity)\nwith g(0)=0 and\n\n    g(cR)=c g(R),                         R>=0.              (1)\n\nExtend it oddly to the real line and set\nf(x)=integral_0^|x| g(R)dR. This gives an even convex differentiable\n1-smooth function with fixed minimum f(0)=0. No prescribed plateau, ramp,\ncurvature pattern, knot count, or sign-crossing history is assumed.\n\nFor every m>=17, every x_0!=0, and the complete GD schedule H_m,\n\n    2f(x_(N_m))/|x_0|^2 <= [1/(a c)]c^(-m).                (2)\n\nThe function g itself may depend on the entire horizon m. Consequently the\nsupremum of these objective ratios over the full class (1), divided by the\nexact U_(N_m)^ZJ, tends to zero. This is a class obstruction, not a bound\non the unrestricted W_(N_m)(H_m).\n\n## 2. A scalar clock and the quantitative closure inequality\n\nThe identically zero g has zero objective. Otherwise g(R)>0 for every R>0:\nif it vanished at one positive radius, (1) and monotonicity would make it\nvanish on arbitrarily large intervals. The continuous ratio g(R)/R is\nperiodic in log R. Thus its maximum M>0 is attained on any multiplicative\nperiod, and M<=1 by smoothness. Put ell=1/M>=1.\n\nDefine a flow clock\n\n    F(R)=integral_1^R dt/g(t),        R>0.\n\nThe arbitrary reference 1 affects only an additive constant. Equation (1)\nimplies that\n\n    F(cR)-F(R)=tau>0                                        (3)\n\nis independent of R: differentiate in R, or substitute t=cu. Choose r>=17\nminimal with b=b_r>=ell. Then b/c<ell<=b. Define\n\n    D=max_(R>0) [F(b g(R))-F(R)].                           (4)\n\nThe expression in brackets is periodic under R->cR, so the maximum exists\non a compact radial period. At a maximizer of g(R)/R=1/ell, one has\nb g(R)>=R. Therefore D>=0.\n\nThe key proved inequality is\n\n    tau+D <=(c-1)b.                                        (5)\n\nHere is a direct proof for every possible input radius; no functional\ninequality is left as an assumption. Fix R>0, put u=g(R)>0 and q=R/u>=1.\n\nIf q<=b, monotonicity gives\n\n    F(bu)-F(R)<=b-q.                                       (6)\n\nOn the period [R,cR], the endpoint gradients are u and cu. Monotonicity and\nthe 1-Lipschitz condition give the pointwise lower bound\n\n    g(t)>=max(u, cu-(cR-t)).\n\nThe switch occurs at t=cR-(c-1)u>=R. Integrating the reciprocal of this\nlower bound yields\n\n    tau<= (c-1)(q-1)+log c.                                (7)\n\nAdding (6)-(7) gives\n\n    tau+F(bu)-F(R)\n       <=b+(c-2)q-(c-1)+log c\n       <=(c-1)b-(c-1)+log c <(c-1)b,\n\nbecause q<=b, c>2 and log c<c-1.\n\nIf q>=b, use (3) to write the same quantity as\n\n    tau+F(bu)-F(R)=F(cbu)-F(R).\n\nWhen cbu<R this quantity is negative. Otherwise every point of integration\nhas gradient at least u, so it is at most cb-q<=(c-1)b. This proves (5)\nuniformly in R, and hence for the actual maximum D in (4).\n\n## 3. Actual block and checkpoint updates of the same clock\n\nEvery step of H_r is at most b/c<ell. Since g(R)<=R/ell, these steps cannot\ncross zero from a nonzero point. For a positive-radius step\nR'=R-hg(R)>0, monotonicity gives\n\n    F(R)-F(R')=integral_(R')^R dt/g(t)\n        >=(R-R')/g(R)=h.                                  (8)\n\nThe same calculation applies after reflecting a negative point, because f\nis even. Therefore each actual H_r block decreases the clock of the absolute\nradius by at least S=s_r.\n\nFor a checkpoint of level k, h=b c^k, consider the actual next radius.\nIf it does not cross zero, its clock decreases. If it crosses, then\n\n    R'=h g(R)-R <= c^k b g(R).\n\nBy (3)-(4), this gives\n\n    F(R')-F(R)<=k tau+D.                                   (9)\n\nSince D>=0, (9) also bounds a same-sign checkpoint. A zero landing has zero\nterminal objective and can be excluded from the clock calculation.\n\nFor r>=17, one has S>=eta b with eta=13/8. To check this directly, note\n(10/13)^3<1/2 and (10/13)^2<4/5, hence (10/13)^17<1/40; substitute in\ns_r/b_r=[1-(2/c)^r]/(c-2). Since eta>c-1=8/5, (5) gives\n\n    S-tau-D>0.                                            (10)\n\nThus the growing-state flow description has a genuine, uniformly profitable\nupdate. It is not just a restatement of the unknown scalar optimization.\n\n## 4. Sum the exact recursive word and retain the original normalization\n\nFirst suppose m>=r and put J=m-r. The word H_m contains 2^J copies of H_r.\nIts checkpoint levels k=0,...,J-1 occur 2^(J-k-1) times. Their total level\nsum is 2^J-J-1, and their number is 2^J-1. Adding (8)-(9) along the full\nactual trajectory gives\n\n    F(|x_(N_m)|)-F(|x_0|)\n       <=-2^J S+(2^J-J-1)tau+(2^J-1)D\n       =-2^J(S-tau-D)-(J+1)tau-D\n       <=-(J+1)tau.                                      (11)\n\nEquation (3) and monotonicity of F imply\n\n    |x_(N_m)|<=c^(-J-1)|x_0|.                             (12)\n\nThe global secant bound g(R)<=R/ell supplies the complete area bound\nf(x)<=x^2/(2ell). Since ell>b/c,\n\n    2f(x_(N_m))/|x_0|^2\n       <=ell^(-1)c^(-2J-2)\n       <=b^(-1)c^(-2J-1)\n       =a^(-1)c^(-m-J-1)\n       <=[1/(a c)]c^(-m).                                (13)\n\nIf m<r, every step in H_m is smaller than ell and the entire trajectory\nstays on its initial ray. Let w=g(|x_(N_m)|) and R=|x_(N_m)|. Monotonicity\nand smoothness give R+s_m w<=|x_0| and f(x_(N_m))<=Rw-w^2/2. Thus\n\n    2f(x_(N_m))/|x_0|^2<=1/(1+2s_m)\n       <=[1/(2eta a)]c^(-m)\n       <=[1/(a c)]c^(-m),\n\nusing m>=17 and 2eta>c. This proves (2) in every case. All distances refer\nto the same fixed minimizer zero. Scaling f by L gives exactly the project's\nnormalization and GD steps h_t/L.\n\n## 5. Explicit exact-profile comparison and finite-knot realizations\n\nFor completeness set C_n=S_circle(n)+1 and D_n=2S_bullet(n)+1. Their shifted\njoins are Omega(A,B)=(A+B+sqrt((A+B)^2+4AB))/2 and\nG(A,D)=2A+D/2+sqrt(D^2+8AD)/2. The bound\nOmega(A,B)<=(A^(3/4)+B^(3/4))^(4/3) reduces to\nt^(4/3)+(1-t)^(4/3)+[t(1-t)]^(4/3)<=1. With x=t^(1/3),y=(1-t)^(1/3),\nthe difference from 1 is x^3 y^3[1/(1+x+x^2)+1/(1+y+y^2)-xy]>=0:\nboth reciprocal terms are at least 1/3 and xy<=2^(-2/3)<2/3. Induction gives\nC_n<=(n+1)^(4/3). Since G(A,D)<=D+4A and the power 4/3 is superadditive on\nnonnegative inputs, the dominant DP gives D_n<=1+4n^(4/3). Multiplying (2)\nby D_(N_m) proves the claimed vanishing ratio, because c^3>16.\n\nIf the profile has finitely many affine pieces within one radial period,\nany one finite nonzero trajectory has a finite-knot realization preserving\nall its values and gradients. Choose a radius R below every nonzero query\nand terminal radius, and put u=g(R)>0 and A=f(R). Smoothness and monotonicity\ngive\n\n    u^2/2<=A<=Ru-u^2/2.\n\nReplace the inner profile by zero up to\ndelta=R-u/2-A/u, then a unit-slope ramp of length u, then a plateau at u up\nto R. The displayed bounds imply 0<=delta<=R-u. Its terminal gradient is u\nand its area is Ru-delta u-u^2/2=A. All larger-radius gradients and values\nare therefore unchanged. Apply the same replacement on both rays and cap\noutside the largest sampled radius. The original finite GD trajectory and\nits exact objective normalization are preserved. This does not claim that\nan arbitrary nonhomogeneous core replacement obeys the same global theorem.\n\nThe new obstruction includes arbitrary legal within-period geometry,\nhorizon-dependent shape and scale, dense gradient values, arbitrarily many\nknots, and every crossing pattern. It is still restricted to even functions\nwith the exact radial period c=13/5. The independently phased staircase\nresult in research/max3_multiscale_flow_obstruction.md remains a separate\nasymmetric extension. Neither result decides the full scalar class or the\nproject's unrestricted all-schedule minimax problem.\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"artifact": "research/max3_general_homogeneous_flow_obstruction.md", "finding_ids": ["1a23bf9f1a7799fd", "24c3252159c7775f", "7e14f9c47de04c17"], "status": "complete candidate proof; unverified; no fact_submit", "scope": "all even discretely homogeneous scalar profiles of radial factor13/5, not full scalar or multidimensional W", "next_gap": "nonperiodic radial geometry or a genuinely general asymmetric flow-state law"}, "glossary": {"M": "Maximum gradient secant g(R)/R, attained on a radial period.", "ell": "Reciprocal of M; at least one.", "F": "Strictly increasing flow clock integral_1^R dt/g(t) for the fixed positive-ray derivative.", "tau": "Constant radial-period clock increment F(cR)-F(R).", "D": "Maximum checkpoint clock increment F(b g(R))-F(R), periodic in the input radius and nonnegative.", "q": "Local reciprocal gradient secant R/g(R), used only in the proof of the closure inequality.", "J": "Number m-r of coarse recursive levels, with b_r the smallest scale at least ell."}}
{"id": "91856e38d10ee82f", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T13:06:45.360600+00:00", "author": "max", "kind": "conclusion", "claim": "Exact enlargement of the geometric state to legal minimum-raising joins: retain the two-parameter sublevel distance Xi(t,r), update it by a one-variable quadratic infimum, and recenter ALL past values by the actual common minimum. In the concave-arc regime the raised minimum has an explicit scalar formula and preserves the outgoing sublevel-domain invariant. A concrete same-Phi/Psi/Dist/G pair shows why Dist at level zero cannot supply the new distance normalization.", "evidence": "# Exact sublevel state for joins that raise the minimum\n\nStatus: UNVERIFIED joint proof and counterexample, worker max, 2026-09-26.\nNo verification call is allocated. This does not assert the unrestricted\nZhang--Jiang comparison. It extends the zero-preserving state test rather\nthan contradicting its explicitly restricted sufficiency statement.\n\n## 1. What the zero-level profile loses\n\nFor a finite convex polyhedral function J on a Euclidean space, write\nenv J(x)=min_z[J(z)+||x-z||^2/2]. All functions below have an attained\nminimum. The unique minimizing z is the proximal point. If x-z is the\ngradient of an affine plane active in J at z, the subgradient inequality\nand the strict quadratic term show that z is the unique proximal point.\nMoreover env J is convex, differentiable, and has 1-Lipschitz gradient\nx-z. One direct proof of the Lipschitz assertion adds the two proximal\nsubgradient inequalities, obtaining\n||g(x)-g(x')||^2 <= <g(x)-g(x'),x-x'>.\nThe minimizing inequalities give differentiability with this gradient.\nFinally min env J=min J and their minimizer sets coincide: equality in\nJ(z)+||x-z||^2/2 >= min J requires x=z and J(z)=min J.\n\nConsider the two functions on coordinates (t,y)\n\n    J_A(t,y)=max(0,1-t,-y),\n    J_B(t,y)=max(0,1-t,-y,3(1-t-y)/4).\n\nBoth have precisely the zero set {t>=1,y>=0}. Start at x_init=(-1,-7)\nand take the one-step prefix h=(7). Both functions have the same legal\nproximal samples:\n\n    query x_0=(-1,-7), proximal z_0=(-1,-6), height V_0=6,\n    gradient g_0=(0,-1);\n    query x_1=(-1,0), proximal p=(0,0), height V_1=1,\n    gradient g_1=(-1,0).\n\nAt z_0 the values of (0,1-t,-y,3(1-t-y)/4) are (0,2,6,6).\nAt p they are (0,1,0,3/4). Thus the selected gradients really are\nactive-plane subgradients for both functions. The GD equality\nx_0-7g_0=x_1 holds. Set u=(1,0), so the same boundary gradient is\n-m_boundary u with m_boundary=1.\n\nThe entire proposed state, including the added gradient magnitude, agrees:\n\n    Phi(t)=max(0,1-t),\n    Psi(s)=min(1,6+s),\n    Dist(t)=(t+1)^2+49 for t>=1, and +infinity otherwise.       (1)\n\nHere Phi(t)=J(p+tu), Psi(s)=min_i[V_i-s<u,z_i-p>], and Dist is\nthe squared distance from the same x_init to the zero slice at t.\nIn fact all prefix queries, gradients, heights, initial points, boundary\npoints, and zero sets agree, a stronger equality than the state requires.\n\nAppend the identical fresh-coordinate cell in a new coordinate w. Choose\nbridge b=3/2, scale d=1/2, cap c=1, endpoint epsilon=1/2, local initial\nquery (epsilon,-1), and no ordinary steps. Its center, common plane\nheight, and two planes are\n\n    p_new=(1/4,0,1/2),   F_cell=13/16,\n    C(t,y,w)=11/16+t/2,\n    E(t,y,w)=1+t/4-w/2.\n\nThese are exactly F_cell+d<t-axis,z-p_new> and\nF_cell+d<(epsilon,0,-1),z-p_new>. For T=A,B define\n\n    Jbar_T=max(J_T,C,E).\n\nThe old query x_1 is sent by the prescribed bridge to x_2=(1/2,0,0).\nAt p_new the two new planes both equal 13/16, while both old functions\nequal 3/4. Therefore g_2=(1/4,0,-1/2) is an active normal and\nx_2=p_new+g_2. The new planes at the two old proximal points have values\n\n    at z_0=(-1,-6,0): C=3/16, E=3/4, both <=6;\n    at p=(0,0,0):     C=11/16, E=1, both <=1.\n\nThis checks both directions of every cross-block comparison. All gradients\nand all GD iterates are actual gradients and iterates of env Jbar_T.\n\nNevertheless neither Jbar_T has a zero. Indeed its minimum is\n\n    mu=19/24,\n\nbecause its planes 1-t and 11/16+t/2 have minimum possible maximum\n19/24, attained only when t=5/24. At that t the endpoint constraint is\nw>=25/48. The remaining minimum-set constraints are exactly\n\n    argmin Jbar_A={t=5/24, y>=-19/24, w>=25/48},\n    argmin Jbar_B={t=5/24, y>=-19/72, w>=25/48}.              (2)\n\nFor B, the extra inequality is\n(3/4)(19/24-y)<=19/24, equivalent to y>=-19/72; it dominates\ny>=-19/24. Every displayed point satisfies all the other planes, proving\nboth attainment and the complete descriptions in (2).\n\nThe actual closest minimizers to (-1,-7,0) are thus the respective corners,\nand their squared distances are\n\n    R_A^2=(29/24)^2+(149/24)^2+(25/48)^2=30931/768,\n    R_B^2=(29/24)^2+(485/72)^2+(25/48)^2=976801/20736.       (3)\n\nBoth have F_cell-mu=1/48>0 and ||g_2||^2=5/16. Their normalized last\nerrors for the identical schedule (7,3/2) are therefore different:\n\n    2(env Jbar_A(x_2)-mu)/R_A^2 =272/30931,\n    2(env Jbar_B(x_2)-mu)/R_B^2 =7344/976801.                (4)\n\nSubtracting the same mu from the WHOLE respective functions normalizes\ntheir minima to zero and changes none of their GD iterates. Every retained\npast function value is reduced by that same constant. This is not an\nindependent reset of a checkpoint value.\n\nConclusion: Phi,Psi,Dist and m_boundary cannot determine the true distance\nnormalization of a minimum-raising join for general allowed polyhedral\nhistories. The previous zero-preserving rule correctly rejects this join;\nits restricted sufficiency is unaffected. The stronger rule that accepts\nall legal joins needs more information than the zero slice.\n\n## 2. The missing information can be written explicitly\n\nDefine the two-parameter sublevel distance profile\n\n    Xi(t,r)=min{||x_init-z||^2:J(z)<=r, <u,z-p>=t},          (5)\n\nwith value +infinity when the set is empty. Extend it by +infinity for\nr<0 when min J=0. The minimum exists whenever feasible: the feasible\nset is closed and the squared norm is coercive.\n\nFor the preceding two histories, for r>=0 and t>=1-r the exact profiles\nare\n\n    Xi_A(t,r)=(t+1)^2+max(0,7-r)^2,\n    Xi_B(t,r)=(t+1)^2+max(0,7-r,8-t-4r/3)^2.              (6)\n\nThey are +infinity outside that domain. These formulas follow by projecting\nthe initial y-coordinate -7 onto y>=-r for A and onto\ny>=max(-r,1-t-4r/3) for B. At r=0 both reduce to (1), while at the\nactual raised minimum r=19/24 and t=5/24 they give the two different\nold-coordinate costs in (3).\n\n## 3. Exact finite update with global recentering allowed\n\nTake any old J_old=max(0,L_1,...,L_M) with min J_old=0. Let its entire\nold ambient space contain x_init, p, u, every old normal and every old\nproximal point z_i. The retained samples have heights V_i=J_old(z_i),\nand the boundary query is p-m_boundary u, m_boundary>0. Retain Phi,\nPsi as in (1) and Xi as in (5). No assumption about a small state size\nis made.\n\nSpecify finite new-cell normals (a,-q), a>0, 0<=q<=1, including a single\nq=0 cap (c,0) and endpoint (epsilon,-1). The local queries (u_j,-y_j)\nand selected normals (a_j,-q_j) obey their prescribed GD differences and\nall local support inequalities, with terminal query and selected normal\n(epsilon,-1). Write\n\n    U_j=u_j-a_j,\n    h_j=< (a_j,-q_j),(U_j,-(y_j-q_j)) > >=0,\n    r_0=(R_x,-R_y),   lambda=sqrt(1+epsilon^2).\n\nA supplied bridge b>1 and scale d>0 determine\n\n    theta=(b-1)m_boundary-d R_x.\n\nAs in the current joining geometry require theta>=0. Choose F_cell>0,\nand a new unit coordinate e orthogonal to the entire old ambient. Put\n\n    p_new=p+theta u+d R_y e,\n    L_(a,q)=F_cell+d<a u-qe,z-p_new>,\n    Jbar=max(J_old,all L_(a,q)),\n    u_new=(-epsilon u+e)/lambda,\n    m_new=d lambda.\n\nThe new query/proximal data are\n\n    x_j=p_new+d(u_j u-y_j e),\n    g_j=d(a_j u-q_j e),\n    z_j=p_new+d(U_j u-(y_j-q_j)e),\n    V_j=F_cell+d^2 h_j.\n\nThe complete two-direction interpolation test is exactly\n\n    max_j[Phi(theta+d U_j)-d^2 h_j] <= F_cell\n       <= min_(a,q)[Psi(d a)+d a theta-d^2 R_y q].         (7)\n\nAn old plane at a new z_j depends only on its old projection\np+(theta+d U_j)u, giving the left side. A new plane at an old z_i is\nF_cell+d a(<u,z_i-p>-theta)+d^2 R_y q, giving the right side.\nTogether with the supplied local inequalities this checks every pair.\nIt gives the proximal data for the same single Jbar, without requiring\nthat Jbar still have a zero.\n\nThe unrecentered outgoing and acceptance profiles are\n\n    Phibar(tau)=max{Phi(theta-epsilon tau/lambda),\n                     F_cell+max_(a,q)[-d(epsilon a+q)tau/lambda]},\n\n    Psibar(s)=min{Psi(-epsilon s/lambda)\n                            +s(d R_y-epsilon theta)/lambda,\n                 min_j[F_cell+d^2h_j\n                            +s d(epsilon U_j+y_j-q_j)/lambda]}. (8)\n\nFor each real r set\n\n    Z_r(t)=max_(a,q),q>0[d R_y+(F_cell-r)/(d q)\n                                      +(a/q)(t-theta)],\n    w(t,tau)=lambda tau+d R_y+epsilon(t-theta).\n\nThe exact sublevel-distance update is\n\n    Xibar(tau,r)=inf_t {Xi(t,r)+w(t,tau)^2:\n        t<=theta+(r-F_cell)/(d c), w(t,tau)>=Z_r(t)}.       (9)\n\nThis follows by writing an enlarged point as z=z_old+w e. Its old\ncoordinate t=<u,z_old-p> must obey J_old(z_old)<=r. The cap gives the\nupper bound on t, each positive-q plane gives one lower bound on w,\nand fixing the new outgoing slice determines w=w(t,tau). Orthogonality\nto x_init as well as the entire old ambient gives the squared-distance\nsum. Conversely an attaining old point for Xi(t,r), with that appended\nw, realizes exactly the displayed cost. Thus (9) is an equality, including\nempty cases; finite minima on fixed new slices are attained by closedness\nand coercivity.\n\nSince Jbar is the maximum of finitely many affine functions, includes zero,\nand is finite, its minimum mu>=0 is attained. One elementary way to see\nattainment is to project its nonempty epigraph polyhedron onto the value\ncoordinate; Fourier--Motzkin elimination gives a polyhedron in R, and\nthe projection is closed and bounded below. Equivalently, using (9),\n\n    mu=min{r>=0: Xibar(tau,r)<infinity for some tau}.       (10)\n\nNow recenter the WHOLE function by J_new=Jbar-mu. Its exact new state is\n\n    Phi_new=Phibar-mu,  Psi_new=Psibar-mu,\n    Xi_new(tau,r)=Xibar(tau,r+mu),  r>=0.                 (11)\n\nAll old and new proximal heights are reduced by the same mu; gradients\nand iterates do not change. As Jbar>=mu everywhere, inserting the zero\nplane into the maximum of all shifted planes leaves J_new unchanged.\nThe boundary gradient magnitude remains m_new. In particular the exact\nnormalized endpoint error is\n\n    [2(F_cell-mu)+d^2(1+epsilon^2)] / min_tau Xibar(tau,mu). (12)\n\nThe denominator is the distance to an actual closest minimizer of the\nsame function whose gradients realize the entire supplied history.\n\nThis is a finite rule. Xi is a convex extended-real piecewise quadratic\nfunction of (t,r): fix any active face of the linearly parameterized\npolyhedron defining (5); its equality-constrained Euclidean projection\nis affine in (t,r), and its squared distance is quadratic. Primal and\nnormal-cone conditions select polyhedral parameter regions. There are\nfinitely many active subsets; if a subset is dependent, retain a basis\nfor the affine hull and describe its normal cone with the full active\nset. This does not require nondegeneracy. On every piece, (9) minimizes\na quadratic in one scalar t over linear inequalities in (t,tau,r).\nThe positive coefficient from the fixed t-slice squared distance permits\nthe unconstrained affine vertex to be clipped to the feasible interval.\nChecking the finitely many pieces gives another finite piecewise quadratic\nprofile. No solver or numerical sweep is part of the construction.\n\n## 4. In the arc-chain regime the amount of recentering is scalar\n\nThere is a useful exact consequence when the old zero-slice domain is\n[F_old/m_boundary,infinity), where F_old=J_old(p)>0. Assume also that\n-m_boundary u is an active old boundary normal. For every\n0<=r<=F_old,\n\n    {t:Xi(t,r)<infinity}=[(F_old-r)/m_boundary,infinity).   (13)\n\nThe supporting plane gives the lower bound. If r<F_old and t is at\nleast that bound, take a zero point with outgoing coordinate\nt_zero=t F_old/(F_old-r), and form the convex combination with p whose\nweight on that zero point is (F_old-r)/F_old. Its coordinate is t and\nits value is at most r. If r=F_old, use p when t=0, and for t>0 mix p\nwith any zero point at a coordinate at least max(t,F_old/m_boundary).\nThis proves every claimed feasible slice without using a distance bound.\n\nAssume the cross-tests (7) hold. Let R_cap=d c. Then the actual minimum\nin (10) is the explicit value\n\n    mu=max(0,[R_cap F_old+m_boundary F_cell\n                  -m_boundary R_cap theta]/[m_boundary+R_cap]). (14)\n\nTo prove it, for any r<=min(F_old,F_cell) the old sublevel is feasible\nat exactly the t in (13). The new cap requires\nt<=theta-(F_cell-r)/R_cap. Every positive-q plane can be satisfied\nby making w sufficiently large. Thus feasibility is equivalent to\n\n    (F_old-r)/m_boundary+(F_cell-r)/R_cap <= theta.\n\nThe displayed root is no greater than F_old because the new cap at p\nis at most F_old. It is no greater than F_cell because the old boundary\nplane at p_new is at most F_cell. These are included in (7), so the\ncalculation applies at the claimed nonnegative minimum itself. This\nproves both the lower bound and attainment in (14).\n\nFor a concave-arc cell satisfying\n\n    a-epsilon q>=0,\n    (a-epsilon q)/c+q>=1,\n\nthe endpoint lower bound on w dominates every other positive-q bound\non the cap-feasible range whenever r<=F_cell. Subtract the endpoint\nbound from a general bound and use\nt-theta<=-(F_cell-r)/(d c); the result is at most\n(F_cell-r)/(d q)[1-q-(a-epsilon q)/c]<=0.\nConsequently the new zero-slice domain after (11) is exactly\n[(F_cell-mu)/(d lambda),infinity). The domain hypothesis used in\n(13) is therefore preserved by these joins, even when mu>0.\n\nIn particular a positive minimum does not make the geometric join\nillegal. It consumes function-value height, with the exact identity\n\n    (F_old-mu)/m_boundary+(F_cell-mu)/R_cap=theta          (15)\n\nwhenever the positive part in (14) is active. The earlier zero-preserving\nbudget was the condition mu=0. Allowing mu>0 requires the sublevel\ndistance state, as the counterexample proves; it does not erase the\nloss or determine its normalization from Dist alone.\n\n## 5. Remaining quantitative question\n\nThis package gives an actual larger class of legal iterative joins and\nan exact, growing finite state. Cross-history legality (7), the loss (14),\nand the nearest-minimizer cost (9) all remain in the same construction.\nA policy selecting cells, scales and levels for every prescribed schedule\nwith normalized endpoint error at least (1-o(1))U_n^ZJ is still absent.\nNone of the displayed example ratios is claimed to optimize W_n.\nAll new assertions here are research findings awaiting verification;\nthey are not accepted graph facts or premises for other submissions.\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"artifact": "workers/max/research/max_sublevel_profile_state.md", "prior_findings": ["97e8798c6b1e9dfc", "6348179e97b86202", "bd45ea19cf2aeaf6"], "status": "unverified joint proof; no new verifier call", "fact_ids": [], "scope": "exact profile-update invariant; uniform B_n comparison remains open", "justification_type": "calculation", "is_fragile": false, "fragility_reason": "", "suggested_followup": "none", "branch_id": "sublevel_state_repair"}, "glossary": {"Xi(t,r)": "Squared distance from the fixed initial point to the old r-sublevel slice with outgoing coordinate t; +infinity for an empty slice.", "m_boundary": "Magnitude of the actual boundary gradient, with gradient -m_boundary u.", "F_cell": "The new cell's common affine-plane height before the common minimum is subtracted.", "R_cap": "New cap-normal magnitude d c.", "mu": "Actual minimum of the enlarged finite polyhedral function before global recentering.", "Phi": "Old affine-plane maximum evaluated on the outgoing line through the boundary proximal point.", "Psi": "Minimum old-query acceptance envelope in the outgoing coordinate.", "Dist": "The zero-level slice distance Xi(t,0)."}}
{"id": "ea3dcd2f7fe4b606", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T13:08:55.761896+00:00", "author": "max3", "kind": "conclusion", "claim": "C15 robust scalar obstruction: actual ray derivatives may be asymmetric and nonperiodic, with arbitrary horizon-dependent knots, provided both lie between (97/100)G_ref and G_ref for one monotone 1-Lipschitz reference satisfying G_ref((13/5)R)=(13/5)G_ref(R). On H_m every such actual trajectory has normalized gap <=(1/(a c))c^(-m), uniformly o(U_(2^m-1)^ZJ). The clock budget tolerates the full 3% loss; no actual-function homogeneity or crossing pattern is assumed.", "evidence": "# A robust scalar clock obstruction with asymmetric nonperiodic derivatives\n\nStatus: complete candidate proof, unverified. No fact_submit is allocated to\nthis lane. Every ingredient used below is proved here; earlier unverified\nfindings are motivation only. This is a bound on a specified scalar function\nclass, not on all scalar functions and not on unrestricted multidimensional W.\n\n## 1. The class and the bound\n\nSet c=13/5, a=c^(-17), b_j=a c^j, and\n\n    H_0=(),   H_(j+1)=(H_j,b_j,H_j),\n    N_j=2^j-1,   s_j=sum H_j=a(c^j-2^j)/(c-2).\n\nLet G_ref:[0,infinity)->[0,infinity) be nondecreasing and 1-Lipschitz,\nG_ref(0)=0, and G_ref(cR)=c G_ref(R) for every R>=0. This is a reference profile,\nnot necessarily the derivative used by GD. Put kappa=97/100.\n\nIndependently choose two nondecreasing 1-Lipschitz functions g_+,g_- on\n[0,infinity), both zero at zero, satisfying\n\n    kappa G_ref(R) <= g_sigma(R) <= G_ref(R),   R>=0, sigma in {+,-}.  (1)\n\nDefine the single fixed function used by the algorithm by\n\n    f(x)=integral_0^x g_+(t)dt                     if x>=0,\n    f(x)=integral_0^(-x) g_-(t)dt                 if x<=0.     (2)\n\nIts derivative is g_+(x) on the positive ray and -g_-(-x) on the negative\nray. It is continuous, nondecreasing and globally 1-Lipschitz: across zero,\ng_+(R)+g_-(T)<=R+T. Thus f is finite, convex, differentiable and 1-smooth,\nwith f(0)=0=min f. It need not be even and neither g_sigma need satisfy\nany radial scaling identity.\n\nFor every m>=17 and every x_0!=0, run GD on this same f with the full\npredetermined schedule H_m. Then\n\n    2 f(x_(N_m))/|x_0|^2 <= (1/(a c)) c^(-m).               (3)\n\nAll choices in (1), including the reference G_ref, may depend on m. In\nparticular, the number of knots, their locations, ray asymmetry and\nnonperiodic changes can depend on the horizon. The constant in (3) is\nindependent of these choices.\n\nThe ratio of the supremum in (3) to the exact U_(N_m)^ZJ tends to zero.\nAn elementary estimate for that exact profile is proved in Section 5.\n\n## 2. Quantitative closure for the reference clock\n\nIf G_ref is identically zero, (1) makes the objective zero. Otherwise G_ref(R)>0\nfor all R>0. Indeed, a zero at one positive radius, monotonicity and discrete\nhomogeneity would force zeros on arbitrarily large intervals. Define\n\n    M_ref=max_(R>0) G_ref(R)/R,    ell_ref=1/M_ref,\n    Clock_ref(R)=integral_1^R dt/G_ref(t),   R>0.\n\nThe ratio G_ref(R)/R is continuous and periodic under R->cR, so its positive\nmaximum exists on a compact radial period. Smoothness gives ell_ref>=1.\nThe clock Clock_ref is strictly increasing. Substitution t=cu gives a constant\n\n    tau_ref=Clock_ref(cR)-Clock_ref(R)>0,                                    (4)\n\nindependent of R. Choose r>=17 minimal with b=b_r>=ell_ref. Thus\n\n    b/c<ell_ref<=b.\n\nDefine\n\n    D_ref=max_(R>0) [Clock_ref(b G_ref(R))-Clock_ref(R)].     (5)\n\nThe expression is continuous and radially periodic, so its maximum exists.\nAt a point attaining M_ref, b G_ref(R)>=R. Hence D_ref>=0.\n\nWe first prove\n\n    tau_ref+D_ref <= (c-1)b.                                     (6)\n\nFix R>0, put u=G_ref(R)>0 and q=R/u>=1. If q<=b, monotonicity gives\nClock_ref(bu)-Clock_ref(R)<=(b-q). On [R,cR], the endpoint values u,cu and the\n1-Lipschitz condition give\n\n    G_ref(t)>=max(u, cu-(cR-t)).\n\nThe switch is at cR-(c-1)u, inside [R,cR]. Integration of the reciprocal\ntherefore yields\n\n    tau_ref <= (c-1)(q-1)+log c.\n\nAdding proves\n\n    tau_ref+Clock_ref(bu)-Clock_ref(R)\n      <= b+(c-2)q-(c-1)+log c < (c-1)b,\n\nbecause q<=b, c>2 and log c<c-1. If q>=b, instead write the expression as\nClock_ref(cbu)-Clock_ref(R). When cbu<R it is negative. Otherwise G_ref(t)>=u on its integration\ninterval, so it is at most cb-q<=(c-1)b. This proves (6) after maximizing.\n\nWe need the slightly stronger block budget\n\n    kappa s_r >= tau_ref+D_ref.                                 (7)\n\nFor r>=18, use (10/13)^18<1/100. One direct exact check is\n13^9=10604499373>10^10, followed by squaring. Consequently\n\n    s_r/b_r = [1-(10/13)^r]/(3/5) > 33/20,\n    kappa s_r/b_r > (97/100)(33/20)=3201/2000>8/5=c-1.\n\nThis and (6) imply (7). If r=17, then b=1 and ell_ref=1. In that case G_ref is\nidentically R: a radius R_0 attaining G_ref(R_0)=R_0 forces G_ref(t)=t on\n[0,R_0] by the Lipschitz bound in both directions; discrete homogeneity\nextends this to every radius. Thus tau_ref=log c and D_ref=0. Also s_17>=13/8:\nindeed (10/13)^3<1/2 and (10/13)^2<4/5 give (10/13)^17<1/40.\nIt follows that kappa s_17>=1261/800>1>log(13/5), proving (7) here too.\nFor the last strict inequality, exp(1)>1+1+1/2+1/6=8/3>13/5.\n\n## 3. Updates for the actual, possibly nonperiodic and asymmetric function\n\nEvery step h within H_r is at most b/c<ell_ref. By (1), g_sigma(R)<=R/ell_ref,\nso such a step preserves the sign and sends R to\n\n    R'=R-hg_sigma(R)>0.\n\nMonotonicity of the reference G_ref and the lower inequality in (1) give\n\n    Clock_ref(R)-Clock_ref(R')\n       >= (R-R')/G_ref(R)\n       = h g_sigma(R)/G_ref(R) >= kappa h.                    (8)\n\nTherefore every full copy of H_r decreases this same clock by at least\nkappa s_r, regardless of which ray it visits or how many gradient knots\nit traverses. This is an estimate on the actual fixed-function trajectory.\n\nA checkpoint of level k has step h=b c^k. If it changes sign, its next\nradius satisfies\n\n    R'=b c^k g_sigma(R)-R <= c^k b G_ref(R).\n\nUsing (4)-(5),\n\n    Clock_ref(R')-Clock_ref(R)<=k tau_ref+D_ref.                                 (9)\n\nIf it preserves the sign its radius decreases, so (9) still holds because\nD_ref>=0. If a step lands at zero, all subsequent iterates are zero and (3)\nholds immediately. No clock is evaluated at zero.\n\nThe same reference clock is used on both rays. There is no assumption\nequating the two actual derivatives, their areas, or their scale periods.\n\n## 4. Sum the exact word and retain the complete objective\n\nSuppose m>=r and put J=m-r and S=s_r. The word H_m contains 2^J copies of\nH_r and 2^J-1 checkpoints. Level k occurs 2^(J-k-1) times for 0<=k<J.\nTheir sum of levels is 2^J-J-1 (obtained, for example, by induction on J).\nSumming (8)-(9) along the original trajectory gives the explicit recurrence\nbound\n\n    Clock_ref(|x_(N_m)|)-Clock_ref(|x_0|)\n      <= -2^J kappa S+(2^J-J-1)tau_ref+(2^J-1)D_ref\n      = -2^J(kappa S-tau_ref-D_ref)-(J+1)tau_ref-D_ref\n      <= -(J+1)tau_ref.                                     (10)\n\nBy (4) and strict monotonicity of Clock_ref,\n\n    |x_(N_m)|<=c^(-J-1)|x_0|.                            (11)\n\nEvery function-value area in (2) is included; (1) gives the global bound\n\n    f(x)<=integral_0^|x| G_ref(t)dt <= |x|^2/(2ell).\n\nAs ell_ref>b/c,\n\n    2f(x_(N_m))/|x_0|^2\n       <= ell_ref^(-1)c^(-2J-2)\n       <= b^(-1)c^(-2J-1)\n       = a^(-1)c^(-m-J-1) <= (1/(a c))c^(-m).\n\nIf 17<=m<r, every step is less than ell_ref. The entire trajectory stays on\nits initial ray with decreasing radius. Write R=|x_(N_m)| and\nw=g_sigma(R). Monotonicity gives\n\n    R+s_m w<=|x_0|.\n\nSmoothness and monotonicity also give g_sigma(t)<=min(t,w) for 0<=t<=R,\nso f(x_(N_m))<=Rw-w^2/2. Completing the square proves\n\n    2f(x_(N_m))/|x_0|^2 <= 1/(1+2s_m)\n       <= (1/(2(13/8)a))c^(-m) <= (1/(a c))c^(-m).\n\nHere s_m>=(13/8)a c^m follows from the calculation for m>=17 above.\nThis completes (3) for every allowed m. Multiplication of f by any L>0\ngives the same normalized ratio and the identical GD trajectory with steps\nh_t/L, so the result uses exactly the project's normalization.\n\n## 5. Comparison to the exact Zhang--Jiang profile\n\nUse the exact sequences in PROBLEM.md, with C_n=S_circle(n)+1 and\nB_n=2S_bullet(n)+1, so U_n^ZJ=1/B_n. Their shifted join maps are\n\n    Omega(A,B)=(A+B+sqrt((A+B)^2+4AB))/2,\n    Gamma(A,B)=2A+B/2+sqrt(B^2+8AB)/2.\n\nFor nonnegative A,B,\n\n    Omega(A,B)<=(A^(3/4)+B^(3/4))^(4/3).                   (12)\n\nTo check (12), normalize A^(3/4)+B^(3/4)=1, write\nA=t^(4/3), B=(1-t)^(4/3), and compare 1 with the positive root of\nz^2-(A+B)z-AB. The needed inequality is\nt^(4/3)+(1-t)^(4/3)+[t(1-t)]^(4/3)<=1. Put\nx=t^(1/3), y=(1-t)^(1/3). Its difference from 1 is\n\n    x^3 y^3 [1/(1+x+x^2)+1/(1+y+y^2)-xy]>=0,\n\nsince each reciprocal is at least 1/3 and xy<=2^(-2/3)<2/3.\nThe case A=B=0 holds directly. Induction in the primitive DP gives\nC_n<=(n+1)^(4/3). Next Gamma(A,B)<=B+4A, by squaring\nsqrt(B^2+8AB)<=B+4A. Induction in the dominant DP, and\nu^(4/3)+v^(4/3)<=(u+v)^(4/3), give\n\n    B_n<=1+4n^(4/3).                                     (13)\n\nThe superadditive power inequality follows, for example, by differentiating\n(u+v)^p-u^p in v for p=4/3 and comparing its derivative to p v^(p-1).\nThus the ratio in question is at most\n\n    (1/(a c)) c^(-m) [1+4(2^m-1)^(4/3)] -> 0,\n\nbecause c^3=2197/125>16. This is an exact-profile comparison, not an appeal\nto a decimal asymptotic coefficient or a computation over finitely many m.\n\n## 6. Explicit deformations and finite trajectory-preserving versions\n\nThe class has genuinely nonperiodic members. Choose measurable functions\nw_sigma(t) in [kappa,1], independently on the two rays, and set\n\n    g_sigma(R)=integral_0^R w_sigma(t) G_ref'(t)dt,\n    f(sigma R)=integral_0^R (R-t) w_sigma(t) G_ref'(t)dt.        (14)\n\nThe derivative G_ref' exists almost everywhere, lies in [0,1], and integrates\nto G_ref because G_ref is Lipschitz. Formula (14) gives (1) and proves the required\nmonotonicity and Lipschitz bounds directly. A piecewise constant w_sigma\nwith arbitrary breakpoints yields independent spatial changes; no periodic\npattern of those breakpoints is required. The choices are made once for the\nentire run and the integral in (14) is the complete function value.\n\nIf G_ref is piecewise affine with finitely many pieces in one radial period,\nand each w_sigma has finitely many pieces on every compact subinterval of\n(0,infinity), any finite trajectory avoiding zero has an equivalent legal\nfinite-knot realization. On each ray choose R below every nonzero sampled\nradius on that ray, and set u=g_sigma(R), A=f(sigma R). Then\n\n    u^2/2<=A<=Ru-u^2/2.\n\nThe lower bound follows from g_sigma(t)>=max(0,u-(R-t)); the upper bound\nfrom g_sigma(t)<=min(t,u). Set delta=R-u/2-A/u. The bounds say\n0<=delta<=R-u. Replace the inner ray by zero up to delta, a unit-slope ramp\nof length u, then a plateau of height u up to R. Its endpoint gradient\nremains u and its area is Ru-delta u-u^2/2=A. All larger gradients and\nvalues are unchanged. If a ray is never queried, choose any such R or\nretain a simple legal zero derivative there. Cap the outer derivative after\nall sampled radii. The two ray replacements join legally at the same\nminimizer zero and preserve the actual GD trajectory, all sampled values,\nand the original initial distance. The number of retained pieces may grow\nwith m. An arbitrary unrelated replacement is not asserted to obey (3).\n\n## 7. Boundary of this result\n\nThis removes evenness and exact radial homogeneity from the actual\nobjective, and it allows unbounded geometric complexity. It retains a\nspecific global restriction: both ray derivatives must lie in the common\nrelative band (1) beneath one discretely homogeneous reference profile.\nIt is not a bound on every asymmetric function or on every nonperiodic\nprofile. A future scalar witness at the benchmark scale must evade this\nuniform band condition (or its trajectory-preserving finite versions).\nThe general scalar question and the all-schedule, all-dimensional exact\nminimax target remain open.\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"artifact": "workers/max3/research/c15_robust_scalar_clock.md", "finding_ids": ["8f56dbb73920cbe5", "7e14f9c47de04c17"], "status": "complete candidate proof; not verified; no routine verifier allocation", "scope": "global relative band around an even homogeneous reference; actual functions asymmetric/nonperiodic; not all scalar functions", "is_fragile": false, "justification_type": "calculation", "confidence": 0.94, "suggested_followup": "none", "next_gap": "large or cumulatively unbounded changes of the radial profile; general scalar interval dynamics"}, "glossary": {"G_ref": "Common reference derivative on the positive half-line, monotone, 1-Lipschitz and discretely homogeneous with radial factor c=13/5.", "g_sigma": "Actual derivative magnitude on ray sigma, independently chosen between (97/100)G_ref and G_ref.", "Clock_ref": "Strictly increasing reference flow clock integral_1^R dt/G_ref(t).", "tau_ref": "Reference radial-period clock increment Clock_ref(cR)-Clock_ref(R).", "D_ref": "Maximum over positive R of Clock_ref(b G_ref(R))-Clock_ref(R), where b is the first schedule scale at least ell_ref.", "ell_ref": "Reciprocal of the maximal reference secant G_ref(R)/R.", "M_ref": "Maximal reference secant G_ref(R)/R.", "w_sigma": "Measurable spatial slope modifier in [97/100,1] giving g_sigma(R)=integral_0^R w_sigma(t)G_ref'(t)dt.", "J": "Number m-r of recursive levels above the coarse block H_r."}}
{"id": "cc1650c148b7676e", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T13:17:20.906217+00:00", "author": "max3", "kind": "conclusion", "claim": "C16 removes the exact-gradient-grid assumption from the multiscale relay obstruction. For every fixed scalar smooth convex function on H_m, if every coarse checkpoint crosses, no H_r crosses internally, and initial-ray H_r blocks are flat, arbitrary nonperiodic plateau heights and arbitrary opposite-ray gradient variation still give normalized gap <=[(b-1)^2/((S+1)^2(2S+1))](1+S/b)^(2-2J)=O(c^(-m))=o(U_(2^m-1)^ZJ). Canonical real plateau intervals force a rank loss of J-1 even with repeated visits and horizon-growing knot counts.", "evidence": "# Arbitrary nonperiodic flat-block relays lose a canonical plateau rank\n\nStatus: complete candidate proof, not verifier-accepted. No new verification\ncall is allocated. The proof is self-contained and does not use earlier\nunverified conclusions as premises. It removes the geometric gradient-grid\nhypothesis from the previous relay obstruction, replacing it by flatness of\nthe positive ordinary blocks. Negative ordinary blocks may vary arbitrarily.\nIt does not cover all scalar functions or unrestricted multidimensional W.\n\n## 1. Statement and the exact class\n\nSet c=13/5, a=c^(-17), b_j=a c^j, and\n\n    H_0=(),   H_(j+1)=(H_j,b_j,H_j),\n    N_j=2^j-1,   s_j=sum H_j=a(c^j-2^j)/(c-2).\n\nFix integers m>r>=18. Put J=m-r, b=b_r, S=s_r, eta=13/8, and\n\n    gamma_rank=1+S/b.\n\nThe schedule H_m is exactly 2^J copies of H_r separated by checkpoints.\nTheir levels form the word\n\n    T_0=(),   T_(j+1)=(T_j,j,T_j),\n\nwhere level ell means step b c^ell. The zero-based even positions in this\ncheckpoint word all have level zero. Its odd positions have levels at least\none. There are 2^(J-1) positive ordinary blocks if the trajectory starts\npositive and every checkpoint changes sign.\n\nConsider any fixed differentiable convex scalar f with 1-Lipschitz\nderivative, f(0)=0=min f, and a GD trajectory starting at x_0>0. Assume:\n\n1. Every displayed checkpoint strictly changes sign, and no step inside a\n   displayed H_r changes sign or reaches zero.\n2. On every positive H_r block, the derivative is one positive constant\n   throughout its traversed interval, including the checkpoint query at its\n   end. The constant may differ between blocks and may be revisited later.\n\nThere is no restriction on the derivative variation within negative H_r\nblocks, beyond legality of the same fixed function and condition 1. There\nis no symmetry, homogeneity, geometric gradient grid, prescribed curvature\npattern, bound on the number of knots, or nested-return assumption.\n\nFor every such trajectory,\n\n    2 f(x_(N_m))/x_0^2\n      <= [(b-1)^2/((S+1)^2(2S+1))] gamma_rank^(2-2J)\n      <= [c/(2 eta^3 a)] c^(-m).                         (1)\n\nThe bound is uniform in r, f, all plateau heights and locations, the number\nof distinct plateaus and every revisit pattern. These may all depend on m.\nThe supremum of the left side over the specified class, divided by the exact\nU_(N_m)^ZJ, tends to zero. Reflection gives the same statement with the two\nrays interchanged, provided flatness is imposed on the initial ray.\n\n## 2. The canonical intervals are intervals of one fixed derivative\n\nIf the terminal gap is zero, (1) holds directly. Otherwise no iterate is a\nminimizer: a zero derivative would make all later iterates identical.\nWrite g_+(R)=f'(R) and g_-(R)=-f'(-R) for R>=0. Both are nonnegative,\nnondecreasing, 1-Lipschitz and bounded above by R.\n\nIndex the positive ordinary blocks in time order by i=0,...,2^(J-1)-1.\nLet u_i>0 be the constant derivative on block i, R_i its final radius\nimmediately before the low checkpoint b, and A_i its initial radius. Then\n\n    A_i=R_i+S u_i,       R_i>=u_i,       R_i<b u_i.          (2)\n\nThe last inequality is exactly the strict sign change at that checkpoint.\nThe full interval [R_i,A_i] has derivative u_i by assumption.\n\nLet U_flat be the finite set of distinct positive numbers occurring among\nthe u_i. For u in U_flat define\n\n    L_flat(u)=min_{i:u_i=u} R_i,\n    A_flat(u)=L_flat(u)+S u.                              (3)\n\nThese are canonical representatives for the whole finite trajectory, not\nnew points chosen independently at later times. The minimum is attained\nat an actual visit. Therefore the interval\n\n    [L_flat(u),A_flat(u)]\n\nis a genuine flat interval of the fixed derivative with value u. Also\n\n    u<=L_flat(u)<bu,     A_flat(u)<(b+S)u.                 (4)\n\nIf u<v are distinct elements of U_flat, monotonicity forces\n\n    A_flat(u)<L_flat(v).                                 (5)\n\nIndeed every point with derivative u lies strictly to the left of every\npoint with derivative v. In particular, this applies to the two endpoints\nin (5). From (4)-(5),\n\n    A_flat(u)<A_flat(v)-S v\n                < A_flat(v) b/(b+S).\n\nThus consecutive canonical radii obey the strict multiplicative separation\n\n    A_flat(v)>gamma_rank A_flat(u)       whenever u<v.    (6)\n\nThis is the source of the discrete rank. The gradient heights themselves\nare arbitrary and need not lie in any geometric grid. Visits at different\npositions on the same plateau are harmless because (3) takes the minimum\nover all of them, once and for all, within the fixed legal function.\n\nFor r>=18 we have S/b>=eta. A direct proof is\n(10/13)^3<1/2, (10/13)^2<4/5, hence (10/13)^17<1/40, and\n\n    S/b=[1-(10/13)^r]/(c-2)>= (39/40)/(3/5)=13/8.\n\nIn particular,\n\n    gamma_rank>=21/8>c,       eta^2=169/64>c.              (7)\n\n## 3. Exact transfer across a pair of checkpoints\n\nConsider two successive positive ordinary blocks, of derivative values\nu and v. The intervening checkpoints have levels (0,ell) with ell>=1.\nLet R be the actual departure radius of the first positive block. After\nthe low checkpoint the negative landing radius is\n\n    B=b u-R <= b u-L_flat(u).                            (8)\n\nLet w>0 and T>0 be the derivative magnitude and radius at the end of the\nnegative H_r block. All earlier negative-block gradients are at least w.\nSmoothness gives T>=w. Thus\n\n    B>=T+S w >= (S+1)w.                                 (9)\n\nThe checkpoint b c^ell lands on the positive ray at the initial radius\nA_next of the next positive block. Equations (8)-(9) give\n\n    A_next=b c^ell w-T\n       <= (b c^ell-1)w\n       <= [(b c^ell-1)/(S+1)] [b u-L_flat(u)].              (10)\n\nThe next block's actual departure is at least L_flat(v), so\nA_flat(v)<=A_next. Since L_flat(u)>=u,\n\n    b u-L_flat(u)\n       <= [(b-1)/(S+1)] [L_flat(u)+S u]\n       = [(b-1)/(S+1)] A_flat(u).                        (11)\n\nFor clarity, multiplying (11) by S+1 and moving the left side to the\nright gives exactly (S+b)[L_flat(u)-u]>=0.\nConsequently,\n\n    A_flat(v)/A_flat(u)\n       <= [(b-1)(b c^ell-1)]/(S+1)^2\n       < c^ell/eta^2 < c^(ell-1).                        (12)\n\nThis transfer uses no negative-ray plateau assumption. It includes all\nnegative-block gradient variation through the lower bound (9).\n\n## 4. A rank decrement that sums over the ruler word\n\nOrder U_flat increasingly, and let rank_flat(u) be its position in this\nfinite ordered set, starting with rank zero. By (6), a positive rank\nincrease of p multiplies A_flat by more than gamma_rank^p.\n\nFor a transition in (12),\n\n    rank_flat(v)-rank_flat(u)<=ell-2.                     (13)\n\nIf ell=1, (12) gives A_flat(v)<A_flat(u), so v<u and the rank decreases\nby at least one. If ell>=2, a rank increase of at least ell-1 would force\nA_flat(v)/A_flat(u)>gamma_rank^(ell-1)>c^(ell-1), contradicting (12).\nZero or negative rank changes already satisfy (13) in this second case.\n\nThe positive-to-positive transitions use precisely the odd-indexed\ncheckpoints of T_J. A level ell>=1 occurs 2^(J-ell-1) times, for\n1<=ell<=J-1. Their rank increments sum to at most\n\n    sum_(ell=1)^(J-1) (ell-2)2^(J-ell-1)=1-J.             (14)\n\nFor J=1 the sum is empty and equals zero. To check (14) for all J, the\ncount is 2^(J-1)-1 and the sum of their levels is 2^J-J-1; subtract twice\nthe former from the latter. Both identities follow by doubling T_(J-1)\nand inserting the new level J-1.\n\nLet u_first and u_last be the derivatives of the first and last positive\nordinary blocks. Equations (6), (13)-(14) imply\n\n    A_flat(u_last)<=gamma_rank^(1-J) A_flat(u_first)\n                        <=gamma_rank^(1-J) x_0.          (15)\n\nFor J=1, the same positive block is both first and last, and the factor\nis one. For J>1, the final rank is at least J-1 below the initial rank,\nso the product of the intervening strict separations (6) proves (15).\nThe last inequality uses A_flat(u_first)<=A_0=x_0, a direct consequence\nof the minimum defining L_flat.\n\n## 5. Complete terminal area and uniformity\n\nThe last checkpoint is again b. Its negative landing radius B_last obeys,\nby (8) and (11),\n\n    B_last <= [(b-1)/(S+1)] A_flat(u_last).                (16)\n\nThe remaining negative H_r has no crossing. Let w=g_-(R) be its terminal\ngradient magnitude and R its terminal radius. All earlier gradients in\nthat block are at least w, so R+S w<=B_last. Integrating the fixed derivative\non [0,R], the bound g_-(t)<=min(t,w) yields\n\n    f(x_(N_m))<=Rw-w^2/2,\n    2f(x_(N_m))<=2 B_last w-(2S+1)w^2\n                          <=B_last^2/(2S+1).             (17)\n\nEvery inner function-value area is present in this inequality. Combining\n(15)-(17) gives the first assertion in (1).\n\nSince S>=eta b, its leading coefficient is bounded by 1/(2 eta^3 b).\nAlso J>=1 and gamma_rank>c, so gamma_rank^(2-2J)<=c^(2-2J). As m=r+J,\n\n    [1/(2 eta^3 b)] c^(2-2J)\n       = [1/(2 eta^3 a)] c^(-m) c^(2-J)\n       <= [c/(2 eta^3 a)] c^(-m),                        (18)\n\nas claimed. This is uniform when r and all function parameters vary with m.\nScaling f by L>0 makes its derivative L-Lipschitz and leaves the trajectory\nunchanged for GD steps h_t/L. The normalized quantity in the project is\nexactly the left side of (1). The fixed minimizer is always zero.\n\n## 6. Exact-profile comparison without asymptotic numerical constants\n\nUse the exact Zhang--Jiang DP definitions of PROBLEM.md. For\nC_n=S_circle(n)+1 and B_n=2S_bullet(n)+1, their shifted joins are\n\n    Omega(A,B)=(A+B+sqrt((A+B)^2+4AB))/2,\n    Gamma(A,B)=2A+B/2+sqrt(B^2+8AB)/2.\n\nThe inequality Omega(A,B)<=(A^(3/4)+B^(3/4))^(4/3) for nonnegative A,B\ncan be checked after normalizing A^(3/4)+B^(3/4)=1. Set\nA=t^(4/3), B=(1-t)^(4/3). Comparing 1 to the positive root of\nz^2-(A+B)z-AB reduces the claim to\n\n    t^(4/3)+(1-t)^(4/3)+[t(1-t)]^(4/3)<=1.\n\nWith x=t^(1/3), y=(1-t)^(1/3), the difference from 1 is\nx^3 y^3[1/(1+x+x^2)+1/(1+y+y^2)-xy]>=0, since the reciprocal terms are\neach at least 1/3 and xy<=2^(-2/3)<2/3. The zero case is direct.\nInduction therefore gives C_n<=(n+1)^(4/3). Next Gamma(A,B)<=B+4A,\nby squaring sqrt(B^2+8AB)<=B+4A. Induction in the dominant recursion\ngives B_n<=1+4n^(4/3), using the superadditivity of the power 4/3 on\nnonnegative numbers. That superadditivity follows by comparing the\nderivatives of (u+v)^p-u^p and v^p in v when p=4/3.\n\nSince U_n^ZJ=1/B_n, (18) divided by U_(N_m)^ZJ is at most\n\n    [c/(2 eta^3 a)] c^(-m) [1+4(2^m-1)^(4/3)] ->0,\n\nbecause c^3=2197/125>16.\n\n## 7. What the construction must change next\n\nThe proposed nonperiodic staircase repair cannot evade this proof by\nchoosing irregular heights, adding more plateau levels, revisiting old\nplateaus, or allowing arbitrary negative-ray curvature. Legal flat\npositive blocks themselves create the separated canonical radii, and the\nsmallest checkpoints force their rank to decrease through the recursive\nword. The exact gradient update is (12) and the integer update is (13).\n\nThe scope restriction is substantive: every coarse checkpoint must cross,\nthere must be no crossing inside H_r, and the initial-ray H_r blocks must\nbe flat through their checkpoint query. A construction with significant\ninitial-ray gradient variation or a different crossing pattern is outside\nthe theorem. No claim about the full scalar class or about W_(N_m)(H_m)\nis made.\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"artifact": "workers/max3/research/c16_arbitrary_plateau_rank.md", "finding_ids": ["ef08dafdd85a7aae", "8f56dbb73920cbe5", "ea3dcd2f7fe4b606"], "status": "complete candidate proof; unverified; no allocated fact_submit", "scope": "one-ray flat coarse blocks, arbitrary other ray, prescribed coarse crossing pattern; no gradient grid or homogeneity", "mechanism": "canonical disjoint plateau intervals force geometric spacing, then exact rank update rank_next-rank<=ell-2", "next_gap": "significant gradient variation inside initial-ray blocks, or altered coarse crossing pattern"}, "glossary": {"U_flat": "Finite set of distinct positive-ray plateau gradient magnitudes on the coarse H_r blocks of the actual trajectory.", "L_flat": "For a plateau height u, the minimum departure radius over all positive blocks with that same height.", "A_flat": "Canonical plateau outer radius L_flat(u)+S u; it belongs to one actual flat interval.", "gamma_rank": "Forced canonical-radius spacing factor 1+S/b, at least 21/8 and hence greater than c=13/5.", "rank_flat": "Zero-based increasing rank of u among U_flat, equivalently of its canonical radius A_flat(u).", "J": "Number m-r of recursive levels above coarse H_r.", "S": "Common sum s_r of the coarse ordinary blocks.", "b": "Common smallest checkpoint a c^r above the coarse blocks.", "T_j": "Ruler checkpoint-level word T_0 empty and T_(j+1)=(T_j,j,T_j)."}}
{"id": "ae9b993c2b8b89dc", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T13:34:28.169598+00:00", "author": "max2", "kind": "conclusion", "claim": "Exact final-stage value candidate for every isolated overrelaxed burst: W_2(a,b)=(a-1)^2 max{1/(1+2b),(b-1)^2} for all a>=2,b>=0. Thus the unique best final step is3/2 and its value is(a-1)^2/4; a fixed explicit R2 function gives quadratic excess against every final b. Zero insertions are included.", "evidence": "# Exact terminal response after a single overrelaxed burst\n\nStatus: self-contained UNVERIFIED candidate. No new fact_submit is allocated.\nOnly accepted facts 1252b8823bb5a290 and b98bb807d514e80d are used as\npredecessors. The one-step identities below are proved inside this candidate.\n\n## 1. Exact statement\n\nUse the original W_n from fact 1252b8823bb5a290: the supremum of\n2(f(x_n)-f_star)/(L||x_0-x_star||^2) over all finite-dimensional convex\nL-smooth functions with a minimizer, for the supplied nonnegative GD schedule.\nDefine, for b>=0,\n\n    r_one(b)=max{1/(1+2b),(b-1)^2}.\n\nFor every a>=2 and b>=0,\n\n    W_2(a,b)=(a-1)^2 r_one(b).                           (1)\n\nConsequently\n\n    inf_(b>=0) W_2(a,b)=(a-1)^2/4,                       (2)\n\nand b=3/2 is the UNIQUE minimizing final step. The same statements hold\nif any number of zero steps are inserted before a, between a and b, or\nafter b. In particular a prefix of any length k>=1 containing exactly\none nonzero step a>=2 has optimized final value (a-1)^2/4.\n\n## 2. One-step upper certificates for every nonnegative step\n\nNormalize L=1 and x_star=g_star=f_star=0. Write x for the initial point,\nu for its gradient, x_1=x-bu, and v for the terminal gradient. Use the\nsigned Q residuals defined in fact1252b8823bb5a290. Every actual instance\nhas all Q<=0.\n\nFor 0<=b<=3/2, put q_one=1/(1+2b). Direct expansion gives the FULL identity\n\n    q_one||x||^2-2f_1+Q_(0,star)+Q_(1,star)+Q_(1,0)\n      =q_one||x-(b+1/2)(u+v)||^2\n         +(3-2b)||u-v||^2/4.                            (3)\n\nFor completeness, the left side after value cancellation is\n\n    q_one||x||^2-<x,u+v>+||u||^2+||v||^2\n                       +(2b-1)<u,v>.\n\nThe right side has the same coefficients, using\nq_one(b+1/2)=1/2. Its residual coefficient is nonnegative in the\nstated range. Thus 2f_1<=q_one||x||^2.\n\nFor 3/2<=b<=2, put r=b-1 in [1/2,1]. Another FULL identity is\n\n    r^2||x||^2-2f_1\n      +2r Q_(0,star)+2(1-r)Q_(1,star)+2r Q_(1,0)\n      =||v+u-(1-r)x||^2+(2r-1)||x-u||^2.             (4)\n\nAll three multipliers and both square coefficients are nonnegative.\nTo check (4), its left quadratic is\n\n    r^2||x||^2-2r<x,u>-2(1-r)<x,v>\n                       +2r||u||^2+2<u,v>+||v||^2.\n\nThe expansion of the right side is identical. Hence\n2f_1<=(b-1)^2||x||^2.\n\nFor b>=2, adding interpolation between x and its minimizer gives\n<x,u>>=||u||^2, while smoothness gives ||u||<=||x||. Therefore\n\n    ||x-bu||^2\n      <=||x||^2+b(b-2)||u||^2\n      <=(b-1)^2||x||^2.\n\nSmoothness at the minimizer then gives the same bound on 2f_1.\nThese arguments include b=0 and both interval endpoints.\n\nThe comparison of the two branches is exact:\n\n    (b-1)^2(1+2b)-1=b^2(2b-3).\n\nThus (3)-(4) and the distance argument prove W_1(b)<=r_one(b) for all b>=0.\n\n## 3. Matching scalar one-step instances\n\nThe scalar quadratic x^2/2 starting at 1 gives normalized error (b-1)^2.\nThe even Huber function with derivative clamp(x,[-q_one,q_one]), where\nq_one=1/(1+2b), gives initial gradient q_one and terminal point\n1-bq_one=(b+1)/(1+2b)>=q_one. Its normalized terminal value is\n\n    2q_one(1-bq_one)-q_one^2=q_one.\n\nBoth functions are globally convex and 1-smooth and minimized at zero.\nThis proves W_1(b)=r_one(b), including b=0.\n\n## 4. The burst factor, with both inequalities\n\nFor any actual function and initial point, the distance argument in Section2\nwith a>=2 gives\n\n    ||x_1-x_star||^2<=(a-1)^2||x_0-x_star||^2.\n\nApply the just-proved one-step upper bound at x_1 for step b. If x_1 is\nalready a minimizer, its subsequent objective gap is zero; otherwise the\none-step bound is directly applicable in its original normalization.\nThus W_2(a,b)<=(a-1)^2 r_one(b).\n\nFor the reverse inequality, fact b98bb807d514e80d applied to the cut at a\nhas prefix sum zero and suffix sum b. It yields\n\n    W_2(a,b)>=(a-1)^2/(1+2b).\n\nIndependently x^2/2 starting at 1 gives exactly\n(a-1)^2(b-1)^2. Taking the larger of these two legal lower bounds proves\n(1). There is no exchange of an infimum with this maximum: the equality\nholds for EVERY fixed b before any minimization is performed.\n\nThe first branch of r_one is strictly decreasing up to b=3/2. After\n3/2 the second branch is strictly increasing. Their common value there\nis 1/4. This proves (2) and uniqueness.\n\nZero steps leave all actual iterates unchanged; their exact compression\nand restoration in the original normalization is part of accepted\nfact1252b8823bb5a290. Therefore all the zero-insertion statements follow.\n\n## 5. A single deterministic two-dimensional saddle instance\n\nThere is also one actual function that resists EVERY final step b and\nattains (2) at 3/2; this is not a choice of scalar instance after seeing b.\nPut A=a-1>=1 and c=A/4. Let B_a(0)=0 and\n\n    B_a'(z)=max(-c,min(1,z)),\n    Q(z)=z^2/2.\n\nThese are convex differentiable 1-smooth scalar functions. At z_0=1,\nboth gradients are 1 and the initial step a sends each coordinate to -A.\nFor B_a the gradient there is -c; for Q it is -A. Define one fixed product\n\n    F(z_1,z_2)=(8/9)B_a(z_1/sqrt(8/9))\n                       +(1/9)Q(z_2/sqrt(1/9)),\n    x_0=(sqrt(8/9),sqrt(1/9)).\n\nIts gradient is 1-Lipschitz in the Euclidean norm, and ||x_0||=1.\nAfter the burst, the scalar final positions are -A+bc and A(b-1).\nAt b=3/2 the B_a coordinate is -5A/8<=-A/4, so the two normalized\nchannel values are both A^2/4. Their derivatives with respect to b\nare respectively -A^2/8 and A^2. Thus the weighted derivative is zero.\n\nThe first channel is convex in b. Taking its tangent at 3/2 and using\nthe exact quadratic second channel gives, for EVERY b>=0,\n\n    2F(x_2(b)) >= A^2/4+(A^2/9)(b-3/2)^2.            (5)\n\nAt 3/2 equality holds. This constructs an explicit final-stage saddle\nin dimension two with a quadratic penalty for deviations from the\noptimal final step. Arbitrary L is restored by replacing F with L F.\n\n## 6. Quantitative state-compression obstruction, sharpened\n\nFor the equal-length, equal-sum prefixes\n\n    h_even=(2,...,2),       length k>=1,\n    h_burst=(2k,0,...,0),   length k,\n\neach step of h_even is distance nonexpansive by the same distance\ncalculation at a=2. Appending 3/2 and using (3) gives\n\n    inf_b W_(k+1)(h_even,b)<=1/4.\n\nThe exact result here gives\n\n    inf_b W_(k+1)(h_burst,b)=(2k-1)^2/4.               (6)\n\nThus the two optimized values differ for every k>=2 and their ratio is\nat least (2k-1)^2. This replaces the older S^(2/3)/12 lower estimate for\nthe burst prefix by its exact value. It is still a restriction on\nsum-only closure, not a general lower bound as a function of the horizon.\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"fact_ids": ["1252b8823bb5a290", "b98bb807d514e80d"], "plan_id": "bb5591b9a9145dfa", "artifact": "workers/max2/research/exact_burst_terminal_response.md", "status": "complete_candidate_unverified", "is_fragile": false, "fragility_reason": "", "suggested_followup": "none", "scope": "subgoal", "justification_type": "calculation"}, "glossary": {"r_one": "Exact candidate one-step response max(1/(1+2b),(b-1)^2).", "q_one": "The reciprocal 1/(1+2b) in the one-step certificate.", "B_a": "Scalar asymmetric Huber function with derivative max(-(a-1)/4,min(1,z)) and B_a(0)=0."}}
